#### Task M9.2.8, fix round 1 (on `44d7dee`)

The controller's rulings, each with its test; each fix shown red by a mutant that undoes it (restored from a copy after each run).
- **I1: decision 23 amended.** The premise that a class's ids only grow is false for reviews: GitHub gives a review its id, and its inline comments theirs, when the pending review is *started*, not when it is submitted. The reviewer's scenario: Alice starts a review (R1, with an inline comment C1); Bob comments (C2) and approves (R2); a view moves the watermark past R1 and C1; Alice submits with changes requested, and the id watermark never sees her review or its thread. **Newness is now decided by key:** a review is new when it is submitted (not `PENDING`) and its id is not in `Watermark.reviews_seen` (the processed review ids, newest `REVIEWS_SEEN_MAX` = 1 000, serde default); a review-thread comment is new when its id is not in its thread's record, `ThreadRecord.comments` (every processed comment: id, author, text; this is also m5). That is stricter than the ruling's "per thread, its last processed comment id": a reply started before, and submitted after, a later reply in the same thread has a lower id than the thread's last one, and the set catches it (`every_fresh_comment_of_a_thread_is_kept_once`). Conversation comments keep the id watermark (a comment's id is given when it is posted, which is when it shows). The three per-class ids remain, now only what I2's page check reads. Tests: `a_review_submitted_after_a_later_item_is_recorded_once` (the scenario; recorded once, never again on later views, nor after `run.json`'s serde, a restart and `run resume`), `every_fresh_comment_of_a_thread_is_kept_once`.
- **I2.** A class read as a full page (`host::VIEW_PAGE` = 100, `THREADS_QUERY`'s `last: 100`) whose oldest id is above the class's id before the view raises the keyed alert `<n>/page/<class>`: `PR #<n>: more than 100 new <comments | reviews | review threads> since the last view; the older ones were not read`; a thread read to `host::THREAD_PAGE` (50, `first: 50`) raises `<n>/page/t<id>`: `PR #<n>: thread t<id> has 50 comments or more; the newer ones were not read` (invented, the ruling's "matching text"). Each is logged once when raised, and the stage's page alerts are replaced on every view, so one clears when a view is no longer full (a thread at 50 stays full, so its line stays). Test `a_full_page_is_an_attention_line_until_a_view_is_not_full` (also pins the page sizes against `THREADS_QUERY`).
- **I3.** `LocalMoved { local }` with `local` the remote head the adopt was for is an adopt re-issued after a restart: it is handled as `Adopted { sha: local }`. Any other `LocalMoved` is a retryable halt, `stage <n>'s branch <local ref> moved outside anthrex: it is at <sha7> | gone, where anthrex had <sha7>; check it, then anthrex run resume <run>`, with the adopt dropped and a view first on resume (no adopt every tick). `Missing` drops the adopt and logs. Tests `local_moved_is_an_adopt_when_it_is_the_remote_head_and_a_halt_otherwise`, `a_missing_remote_branch_drops_the_adopt`.
- **Concern 3.** Decision 24's halt (an adopt's `NotDescendant`, and an update push's `Rejected`, which cannot tell a push from a rewrite either) reads `remote stage branch anthrex/<run>/stage-<n> moved: someone else pushed to it or rewrote it; stage <n> (PR #<pr>) is at <sha7> on <remote>, which does not contain <sha7>, and anthrex never forces a push` (the push: `…; stage <n> (PR #<pr>) could not fast-forward it to <sha7>, and anthrex never forces a push`). This replaces the Messages table's `remote stage branch was rewritten by someone else` for these two halts; task 7's opening-push `Rejected` keeps TT's text (TT §6.2's case, tested in task 7). The Messages log line stays.
- **m1.** A stage's update push, and its opening push, wait while any lower stage is held (`watch::lower_held`): its unpushed commits would ride in the upper PR's diff. Test `a_held_lower_stage_holds_the_push_above`. **Carried to M9.2.11:** landing a held stage never counts its unpushed head as landed.
- **m2.** The `auth` alert clears only on the success of a `gh` op (not `Push`, `Fetch` or `DeleteBranch`, which are `git`). Test `only_a_gh_answer_clears_a_lost_login`.
- **m3.** Each pass, a stage's `push` and `fetch` failures and their alerts are dropped once that op is no longer due (the stage head is the pushed head; no adopt due). Test `a_push_no_longer_due_drops_its_failures_and_the_line_is_never_repeated` (also m9: past five failures the line is shown once).
- **m4.** `failed` one-lines the host's error text once (`safe_text::one_line`) before it is cut to 200 and used in the log and the alert; a hold's reason is one-lined and cut to 300 characters before it is kept. Test `host_text_is_one_lined_and_cut`.
- **m5.** See I1: every fresh comment of a thread is kept in `ThreadRecord.comments`, in the order read; `text` stays the newest. For the controller: a thread of 50 comments of 8 000 characters keeps 400 KB in `run.json`; M9.2.10 may drop the text of comments it has acted on.
- **m7.** A view answering another PR's number is logged (`stage <n> (PR #<pr>): the host answered for PR #<m>; ignored`) and the PR is polled again on its backed-off interval. Test `an_answer_for_another_pr_is_logged_and_polled_again`.
- **m8.** Decision 11's doubling is **per rate-limited answer**: each `RateLimited` answer doubles `poll_base_secs` (at most 3 600), so two stages limited in the same second double it twice; any success restores `poll_secs` (`rate_limit_doubles_the_interval_until_a_success`).
- **m9.** `a_failed_fetch_waits_for_its_retry` (an adopt's fetch failing waits `retry_at`, then is re-issued); the no-repeat check is in m3's test; `restore_replays_no_event_twice` now runs with an orchestrator (9.1's `bisect::with_orchestrator`) and compares its notes after the resume (which adds the restart's own note) with its notes after the replayed view.
- **Red.** 13 mutants, each undoing one fix, each red against its test: reviews new by id again; thread comments new by id again; comments not kept; no page check; a re-issued adopt not adopted; another `LocalMoved` not halting; a held lower stage ignored; any success clearing `auth`; no settling of failures; the hold reason and the error text not one-lined; a wrong-PR view not rescheduled; a fetch's `retry_at` ignored. `rewritten_remote_halts_with_the_exact_text` was red on the new text before its expectation changed.
- **Commands:** `cargo test -p anthrex-daemon --lib` (2369 pass), `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo +1.99 clippy -p anthrex-daemon --all-targets -- -D warnings` (host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`), `cargo +1.98 check -p anthrex-daemon --all-targets`: clean; the purity grep prints nothing. No `gh` ran; no daemon was started.
- **Line counts:** `engine/delivery/mod.rs` 371, `open.rs` 251, `view.rs` 370, `watch.rs` 491; `tests/delivery_watch.rs` 557, `delivery_watch_adopt.rs` 465, `delivery_watch_fixes.rs` 421 (new); `run/delivery/mod.rs` 350, `run/delivery/tests.rs` 536, `host/mod.rs` 418. `propagate.rs` and `merge.rs` were not touched, so no move commit was needed.

### Task M9.2.9

- **What exists now.** `decider/ci.rs` (pure): `CiSummaryInput`, `CI_SUMMARY_INPUT_BYTES`, the exact prompt head, `prompt` (the log's last 48 KiB through `quote::ci_log`), `parse`, `fallback` (the log's last 40 lines, `[]`, `unknown`), `safe_test_name`/`safe_tests` (decision 18's pattern). `DeciderKind::CiSummary` appended (`ALL` has 5), `DeciderRequest::CiSummary`, `DeciderAnswer::CiSummary`, one arm each in `fallback.rs`, `prompt.rs`, `parse.rs` and the schema in `schema.rs` exactly as Interfaces gives it; `CheckSummary` is unchanged. `run/engine/delivery/` (pure): `ci_trigger.rs` (the trigger, moved from `view.rs`, and the record a red starts), `ci.rs` (the state machine: logs, summary, cap, infra re-run), `ci_repro.rs` (step 4: reproduction and the hand-off to 9.1's bisect), `fix.rs` (decision 26 for CI: the `ci` template, owns, routes, `tasked`/`to_user`). One routing row in `results.rs` (a CI reproduction's `TestAt` or tier-2 answer), one in `deciders.rs` (a `ci_summary` answer), two in `delivery/mod.rs` (`Logs`, `Rerun`, and a failed re-run released for retry). 9.1's `bisect.rs` (+10): a CI bisect's culprit goes to `delivery::ci_culprit` and is not charged to `bisect_fixes`; `bisect_end.rs::end_with` sends a CI bisect's no-culprit end to `delivery::ci_no_culprit` instead of 9.1's attention line and wake note.
- **The carried rulings.**
  - *CiSeen keys by job:* `CiSeen.jobs` (serde default) holds each red check's `<run>/<job>` from its `detailsUrl` (a check with no job: its name); a head's red is new when its job set is new, so the red after a re-run (new job ids) is seen. A `CiSeen` written by task 8 (no `jobs`) compares by names. **`FakeGh` was changed to match GitHub:** `gh run rerun --failed` now gives the run's job a new id (documented GitHub behaviour, not observed here: for the manual check).
  - *No second re-run after a restart:* each `RerunFailed` is recorded in `CiRecord.reruns` in the step that emits it; a failed re-run (`HostResult::Error`) is removed again so it retries. When every re-run was issued, none is in flight and one was never answered (its answer lost in a restart), the red is handled as `unknown` at once, never re-run (`stage <n>: a re-run's answer was lost in a restart; CI red at <sha7> is unknown, not re-run again`). Tested with a restart between the issue and its record.
  - *`CI on <sha7>`* for an empty run list: task 6's `fix_text`, unchanged.
- **Deviations** (none changes a design decision; each forced or a reading the brief left open):
  1. **The log's text reaches the engine through the host's answer, not a driver read.** Decision 18 has the driver fill `CiSummaryInput.log` from the file. But the engine (pure, no file reads) needs the text itself: for the deterministic fallback, which it computes in three places (deciders off, no reader slot in time, a failed call), and for the fix task's brief (`quote::ci_log` of the last 200 lines), which `add_fix` writes in the engine. So `host::LogFile` gains `#[serde(default)] tail: String`, the file's last `CI_SUMMARY_INPUT_BYTES` that `GhHost::failed_logs` already holds in memory (so `FakeHost` gives it too), and the engine fills `log` itself. Each `FailedLogs` answer in the journal is at most 48 KiB; `CiRecord.text` (at most 48 KiB in all) is dropped once the record is tasked or handed to the user. No driver change.
  2. **`CiRecord` gains working fields** (each `#[serde(default)]`, not in Interfaces): `checks`, `jobs`, `external`, `infra_only`, `fetched`, `text`, `decider`, `source`, `reruns_answered`, `probe`, `probe_failures`, `retry_at`, `command`; `CiRecord::new(head)`.
  3. **Logs:** one `FailedLogs` per Actions run (at most 5), each capped at `ci_log_max_bytes / runs` so the whole stays under the cap; a check with no Actions run adds `<name>: <CONCLUSION> (<url, one line, 300 chars>)` after the logs. A red of cancellations and startup failures only fetches no log before its re-run; the red after the re-run fetches them.
  4. **The cap is checked when the summary is in, before an infra re-run**, so `ci_fix_max = 0` hands every red to the user at once, infra included. `<k>` counts every `ci` fix task of the stage with that key. An infra red's key is `infra` until it becomes `unknown`.
  5. **After the re-run, only `infra` becomes `unknown`:** the decider is asked again on the second red's logs; a `test` answer with names is reproduced with them.
  6. **Reproduction:** names first (`single_test` per name, at most 10, when the profile has `single_test`), else `build_check`/`check` for build or lint, else 9.1's tier-2 steps as an `OpKind::Tier { tier: 2 }` job in `.full` with a scratch checkout at the PR head, `diff_base` the stage below's head (else the stage's creation point), priority `FullStage`, task none (the executor runs it as written: untiered, `check`). A bisect follows only for names on a tiered profile. With nothing to run (no `build_check` or `check`; no check gate) the stage fix says `It was not reproduced locally: <reason>.` (invented: the template has two choices, and TT's environment sentence would be untrue); the same after three executor failures of the probe (`the local run failed: <first line>`), retried after 30 s and 120 s. `<command>` in `It reproduces locally with:` is the failing commands joined by `; `, or the failed tier step's command.
  7. **A probe lost in a restart** is found by the pass (its op is no longer pending), `full_op` released, and issued again; `restore.rs` is not touched.
  8. **Stale records:** a record whose PR is no longer open, whose stage is paused, or whose head is no longer the pushed head is dropped with `stage <n>: CI red at <sha7> is not acted on: <why>`, except while its probe or its bisect is in flight (each re-checks when it answers). A green view of the pushed head removes a record waiting on its re-run and clears the stage's CI attention lines; so does a merged or closed PR.
  9. **Approval:** a CI fix never needs `HoldKind::Fix` (left to M9.2.10, which owns it): a culprit fix copies an approved stage task's `owns` exactly and a stage fix owns the union of the stage's approved `owns`, so every entry is covered by construction. "Approved" is `gate_holds::released` and not cancelled. The stage fix's epic is none.
  10. **Routes:** culprit: one rung up, then its own route, then the policy's (`RouteSpec::default()`); stage fix: the strongest, then the policy's. When every one is refused the red goes to the user.
  11. **Texts the Messages table leaves open** (invented): the attention line `stage <n> CI red (<category>): sent to you; its fix task was refused: <message>` with the wake note `stage <n> CI red (<category>): sent to you`; log lines `stage <n> (PR #<k>): CI red again at <sha7> after its re-run`, `stage <n>: CI red at <sha7> was cancelled or did not start: infra`, `… is infra; re-running its failed jobs once`, `stage <n>: re-ran the failed jobs of CI run <id>`, `stage <n>: the CI summary's failing tests: <k> dropped (not test names)` (a count, never the names), `stage <n>: reproducing CI red at <sha7> with <command>`, `… reproduces; bisecting <k> merges`, `… reproduces; not bisected: <reason>`, `… does not reproduce locally`, `stage <n>: CI red at <sha7>: no culprit: <reason>`, `stage <n> (PR #<k>): CI green at <sha7> after its re-run`. The brief's `<source>` is `decider` or `fallback (<reason>)` (`none` when no summary was made); each summary line is one-lined before it is indented.
  12. **Files beyond the list:** `ci_trigger.rs` and `ci_repro.rs` (`ci.rs` passed 800 lines after `cargo fmt`; rule 8); the engine tests are three files (`delivery_ci.rs`, `delivery_ci_cap.rs`, `delivery_ci_repro.rs`), the decider's one. `host/mod.rs`, `host/gh.rs`, `host/tests_parse.rs`, `host/fake/{rules,tests_ci_merge}.rs` (deviation 1 and the re-run job id), `run/delivery/{mod,tests,tests_snapshot}.rs` (the model fields), `engine/tests/delivery_watch.rs` (`poll_with` answers a pending `FailedLogs` with an empty log first: a red view now starts one, and it takes the stage's one host op). M8b's `schemas_match_the_brief` now checks M8b's four kinds (`ALL[..4]`) against M8b's brief; `ci_summary` is checked against this brief in `tests_ci.rs`. `decider/parse.rs`'s four checkers became `pub(super)` for `ci.rs`; `parse.rs` +10, `mod.rs` +19 (rustfmt's multi-line variants), both over their budgets of +3 and +12, far from 600.
- **Tests** (19 new or extended): `decider/tests_ci.rs` `ci_summary_schema_prompt_and_fallback_are_exact`, `check_summary_is_unchanged` (pinning), `ci_summary_drops_unsafe_test_names`; `engine/tests/delivery_ci.rs` `red_head_fetches_logs_then_summarises`, `pending_checks_wait`, `a_stale_head_is_ignored`, `an_external_check_contributes_its_line`, `cancelled_only_is_infra_without_a_decider`, `infra_red_reruns_once_then_counts_as_unknown` (with the restart), `ci_fix_task_brief_quotes_the_log_as_data` (the whole brief, exact); `delivery_ci_cap.rs` `ci_fix_max_sends_the_same_red_to_the_user`, `another_failing_set_still_gets_a_task`, `ci_fix_max_zero_sends_every_red_to_the_user`; `delivery_ci_repro.rs` `reproduce_runs_only_the_failing_tests_at_the_pr_head` (and a probe lost in a restart), `build_and_lint_reproduce_with_build_check`, `reproduced_with_a_culprit_adds_a_ci_fix_with_the_culprits_owns_one_rung_up`, `reproduced_without_a_culprit_adds_a_stage_fix_with_the_strongest_route` (untiered, a bisect with no culprit, an adopted commit), `not_reproduced_adds_an_environment_fix_task_with_tts_sentence`; extended: `host/tests_parse.rs::failed_logs_keeps_the_head_and_the_tail_under_the_cap` (the tail), `host/fake/tests_ci_merge.rs::fake_ci_rules_fail_on_file_content` (the tail; a re-run's new job id).
- **Red.** The code was drafted before the engine tests ran, so each test was shown to bite by mutation: 20 mutants, each applied to a copy, run against its tests and restored, all red: no record from a red view; `CiSeen` by names; cancelled-only fetching logs; a re-run recorded only when answered; infra kept after the re-run; the cap off by one; names reproduced with `check`; build with `check`; never bisected; a culprit given 9.1's bisect fix; the no-culprit end left to 9.1; the first task's route; the log not quoted by `ci_log`; unsafe names kept; never stale; a lost probe waited for; external lines dropped; no tail from the host; a fake re-run keeping its job; an empty fallback.
- **Commands run:** `cargo test -p anthrex-daemon --lib` (2387 pass), `cargo test -p anthrex-fake-agent`, `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo +1.99 clippy -p anthrex-daemon --all-targets -- -D warnings` (host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`), `cargo +1.98 check -p anthrex-daemon --all-targets`: clean. The purity grep prints nothing for `run/delivery`, `run/engine/delivery` and `decider/ci.rs`. No `gh`, `claude` or `codex` ran; no daemon was started.
- **Line counts:** `engine/delivery/ci.rs` 391, `ci_repro.rs` 266, `ci_trigger.rs` 213, `fix.rs` 380, `delivery/mod.rs` 389, `view.rs` 329; `decider/ci.rs` 137, `tests_ci.rs` 248, `mod.rs` 289, `schema.rs` 43, `prompt.rs` 434, `parse.rs` 332, `fallback.rs` 70; `engine/deciders.rs` 433, `results.rs` 127, `bisect.rs` 560, `bisect_end.rs` 92; `tests/delivery_ci.rs` 517, `delivery_ci_cap.rs` 130, `delivery_ci_repro.rs` 343, `delivery_watch.rs` 571; `run/delivery/mod.rs` 424, `tests.rs` 551; `host/mod.rs` 422, `gh.rs` 462, `tests_parse.rs` 593; `fake/rules.rs` 267, `tests_ci_merge.rs` 318.
- **Fix round 1** (the controller's rulings on task 9's review):
  - *I1 (decision 22):* a fallback summary (deciders off, no slot, a failed call) is the log's own last 40 lines, so the brief no longer repeats it outside the fence: under `Summary of the failure (…, fallback (<reason>)):` it prints `  (none: see the quoted log)`, and the quoted log carries the lines. **A decider's own summary lines stay outside the fence by design**: they are the decider's words, one-lined and cut, not the log. Every kept line is cut to 300 characters in every path (in `ci::summarised` and again in the brief).
  - *I2:* tests for a green view of the pushed head after the re-run (the `Rerunning` record removed, `… CI green at <sha7> after its re-run` logged), and for a red whose PR is merged or closed, or whose stage pauses, while its summary is written (dropped with its `not acted on` line; no fix task, no probe, no attention line).
  - *m2:* `ci::gone` = `stale`, or 9.1's `full::ending` (`the run is ending`). It is checked by the pass's step and at the top of `summarised` and `reproduced` (and by the culprit and no-culprit ends), so a merge, close or cancel during the decider call or the probe adds nothing and raises no attention line that nothing would clear.
  - *m3:* `FailedLogs` failures for a run count in `CiRecord.log_failures` (serde default); after `FAILURES_BEFORE_ATTENTION` in a row the run is given up (`stage <n>: the failed log of CI run <id> could not be fetched after 5 tries; summarising without it`) and the summary is made without its log.
  - *m4:* `ci_culprit` returns `CiCulprit` (`Fixed`, `NoCulprit(reason)`, `Refused(reason)`); 9.1's `culprit` records its history result. A culprit merge that is not a task gets the stage fix and the history reason `the culprit <id> is not a task` (not a refused culprit); a red that stopped mattering, `CI red not acted on: <why>`. `bisect.rs` is 558 lines (it was 560).
  - *m5:* a `RerunFailed` error whose text says `already running` is handled as `HostResult::Rerun` in `host_done` (decision 10, in the engine too: no failure count, no re-issue). A timed-out re-run is issued again once (`CiRecord.rerun_timeouts`, serde default); after a second timeout it stays issued and unanswered, and the red is `unknown` (`stage <n>: a re-run timed out twice; CI red at <sha7> is unknown, not re-run again`, invented). Any other error still issues it again.
  - *m6:* the cap's attention line shows the key's first 120 characters, then `…`; the record keeps the whole key.
  - *m7:* `delivery_watch.rs::poll_with` explains its auto-answer of `FailedLogs`.
  - *Ruling 43:* a held stage may get a CI fix task. The run keeps its other work, and the fix task's commit waits for the release like any held stage's.
  - Tests (9 new): `delivery_ci_edges.rs` holds `a_fallback_summary_stays_inside_the_fence`, `a_green_view_after_the_rerun_removes_the_record`, `a_red_whose_pr_lands_or_pauses_is_dropped`, `a_cancel_during_the_summary_or_the_probe_adds_nothing`, `failed_logs_that_keep_failing_are_summarised_without`, `a_rerun_already_running_is_accepted`, `a_timed_out_rerun_is_issued_again_once` and `the_cap_line_cuts_a_long_key`. `delivery_ci_repro.rs` holds `a_ci_bisect_records_what_happened`. `run/delivery/tests.rs`'s round trip covers the two new fields.
  - Red: the seven tests for changed behaviour failed first, each on its assertion. The two I2 tests cover behaviour that already worked, so they passed before the change; mutants M21–M23 (the green-view removal; the merged/closed and paused checks in `stale`) each turned them red. M24–M26 did the same for the new code: the fallback lines repeated, `ending` ignored, and `reproduced` without `gone`.

### Task M9.2.10

- **What exists now.** `run/engine/delivery/` (pure): `review.rs` (decision 29's intake of a view's fresh comments, who counts and the one-at-a-time `Permission` op, decision 31's batches, the cap, the wake, the fast path, the attention lines, and the bound on kept review text), `review_fix.rs` (the template's fix task, decision 26's `HoldKind::Fix` for it and for a plan edit's `addresses` task), `reply.rs` (decision 30's automatic replies, `reply_comment`'s sending, the answers). `run/delivery/` (pure): `validate.rs` (`PlanTask.addresses` and the merged/closed-stage refusal), `reply_edit.rs` (`reply_comment`, the marker, the safe reply body), `templates.rs` (Interfaces' `review` template). `view.rs` hands each fresh comment's id and author to the intake and trims at the end of a view; `engine/delivery/mod.rs` runs the review pass after CI's and before the watch pass, routes `Permission` and `Replied`, and retries a failed `Permission`; `watch.rs` tells the replies of a push sent, landed, and of an adoption (three one-line calls) and adds the review attention lines; `engine/orch.rs` (+2) holds an orchestrator's out-of-stage review fix after `gate_holds::assign`; `edits.rs` (one arm, two lines in `resolve`), `edits_orch.rs` (`reply_comment` in the one-edit rule), `orch/contract.rs` (`ONE_EDIT_RULE`'s text, its test), `gate_holds.rs` (`create` is `pub(super)`).
- **The carried rulings.**
  - *Filters* (task 8's): bots (GitHub's `Bot` type, or a login ending `[bot]`) are filtered before anything else, and a login `remote::owner_ok` refuses is never asked about (the allow-list would refuse the `Permission` argv): it does not count, and nothing halts (`only_writers_and_listed_reviewers_count`, login `-x`).
  - *anthrex's own comments are known by identity, not by text.* A comment is anthrex's when its id is one a reply was answered with (`StageDelivery.own_comments`), or when it holds the exact marker of a reply anthrex **sent and has had no answer for** (a restart or a timeout lost it; the id is then recorded). A reviewer who pastes a marker, or any `<!-- anthrex:` text, is still a reviewer (`bots_and_the_anthrex_marker_are_ignored_but_the_users_own_comments_count`). This replaces decision 29's "a body containing `<!-- anthrex:` is anthrex's" (the controller's ruling). A stage's due reply is sent before its next view, so the id is normally known before a view can show the comment.
  - *Bounded `run.json`*: a thread keeps every comment's id (newness is by id), the text of only its newest 20 comments, each cut to 4 000 characters, and only while it is `new`; a stage keeps at most 200 000 characters of review text in all, newest threads first (`trim`, run at the end of each view). `review_text_kept_in_run_json_is_bounded`: 40 threads of 50 comments of 8 000 two-byte characters and 100 such conversation comments in one view leave `run.json` under 1 MiB and the snapshot frame under 64 KiB.
  - *No feedback loop*: a reply's own comment is ignored as anthrex's own (logged), so it never counts, never wakes the orchestrator and never makes a task.
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision beyond the ruling above):
  1. **Files.** `review.rs` passed 800 lines after `cargo fmt`, so the template's task and the hold are `review_fix.rs` and the replies `reply.rs` (rule 8); the template is `run/delivery/templates.rs` (the "File sizes" note). Three test files: `delivery_review.rs`, `delivery_review_fix.rs`, `delivery_review_reply.rs`.
  2. **`HostOp::Reply` gains `#[serde(default)] marker`.** The marker's `<sha7>` is the engine's to know (the fix task's merge commit), so the engine builds it and M9.2.12's executor passes it as `ReplyReq.marker`.
  3. **Model fields** (each `#[serde(default)]`, not in Interfaces): `ThreadRecord.{candidates, counted, batch}`; `StageDelivery.{replies: Vec<ReplyDue>, auto_replies, own_comments, batches, round_batch}`; `RunDelivery.permission_retry_at`. `ReplyDue { thread, target, body, marker, task, push, ready, sent }`. `Batch.threads` holds keys.
  4. **When an automatic reply goes.** A merged review fix queues one reply per thread once (`auto_replies`, `"<task>/<key>"`); the push that next goes out tags it, and its `Pushed`/`UpToDate` makes it due. An adoption (decision 24) makes every waiting reply due: the remote descends from the stage head, which holds the merge. `reply_to_comments = false` queues none; the threads stay `tasked`. A reply answered `NotFound` is dropped (logged); any other error retries at the stage's `retry_at`.
  5. **`reply_comment`.** `thread` is the key (`c5`) or the ref (`7:c5`); the edit log names it `<pr>:<key>` either way (`edit_log.rs`, one arm). Its marker's `<sha7>` is seven hexadecimal digits of an FNV-1a hash of the key, the time and the body (it has no fix commit), kept with the reply so a restart re-sends the same marker. The body is made safe before it is kept: hidden format characters dropped, other controls but `\n` made spaces, `@` turned into `＠` (no mention), `<!--` written `&lt;!--` (no forged marker, which `GhHost::reply`'s marker check would take for a reply already posted). Refusals in the brief's order (PR, thread, body, turned off); a sub-planner's: `a sub-planner cannot reply to a comment` (invented, like M9's message refusal). The thread is `replied` (`comment_id: 0`) at once and gets its id when answered.
  6. **`addresses`** is checked in `Batch::resolve`, so for `add_task` and `split_task`, for every source; a sub-planner's is refused (`task <id>: a sub-planner cannot address review threads`, invented). A ref is `<pr>:<key>` only, of a thread that is `new` (counted or still waiting for its author's permission), distinct within the task. `stage <n>'s PR is <merged | closed>; add the task to a later stage` refuses **any** task added to such a stage in `pr` mode. A plan file's `addresses` at `run start` is not checked (no PR exists then).
  7. **Batches** do not close while a thread of the stage waits for its author's `Permission` answer, so a slow answer never splits one review into two wakes (`a_batch_waits_for_a_pending_permission`). A failed `Permission` op is asked again one poll interval later (`a_failed_permission_is_asked_again_when_due`). A batch of a PR that is not open, or of a paused stage, closes with no task and no wake (its threads stay `new`).
  8. **Review rounds.** A fast-path batch that made a task is a round; for a planned run, the first `addresses` task naming a thread of a closed batch not yet counted makes that batch a round.
  9. **Fast path.** "A thread whose fix task already exists never gets a second one": a thread with a fix task that is not cancelled stays `new` and is listed as not addressed. Its brief quotes only the comments of authors known to write (the counted author, a listed reviewer, a cached writer): a comment by anyone else in the same thread never reaches an agent (`comment_text_never_sets_owns_route_or_id`). A path that is not a literal path inside the repository (`validate_glob` of the escaped path, on one line, at most 1 000 characters) is PR-level. A refused fix (every route) is the attention line `PR #<pr>: thread <ref> by @<login> is yours; its fix task was refused: <message>` (invented).
  10. **Plurals.** The wake note reads `has 1 new review thread from …` and the attention line `PR #<pr>: 1 thread not addressed` for one (the brief's texts for two or more), as task 7 pluralised the cancel outcome. The unaddressed line is shown for open PRs only.
  11. **Invented log lines**: `stage <n> (PR #<pr>): review batch <b>: <keys>`, `stage <n> (PR #<pr>): replied on <pr>:<key>`, `stage <n>: the reply on thread <key> was dropped: not found`; the ignored line is the brief's with the `stage <n> (PR #<pr>): ` prefix every stage line has.
  12. **The orchestrator's argument bounds** (`orch/tools_bounds.rs`, M9.11's raw-argument check) gain `addresses` on a task and `reply_comment`'s `pr`, `thread` and `body`: without them the daemon refuses both before serde reads them. The MCP schemas stay M9.2.13's.
  13. `delivery_watch.rs`'s `view_answer` picks the pending view among the host ops (a run-level `Permission` op may be out beside it).
- **For M9.2.12:** pass `HostOp::Reply.marker` as `ReplyReq.marker`. **For M9.2.13:** the digest's quoted `comment` should use the same writer filter as the fast path's brief (a review thread's `text` is its newest kept comment, which may be a non-writer's). **For the controller:** `GhHost::reply`'s marker check (task 4) takes any comment holding the marker for the reply already posted, whoever wrote it; a reviewer who pasted the exact marker of a reply anthrex is about to post (it names the fix's merge commit) would suppress it. And a `PrViewed` answer can carry up to 100 threads of 50 comments of 8 000 characters into the journal (the view's own bound, task 4/8), far more than what the model now keeps.
- **Fix round 1** (the controller's rulings on the review; commits on top of task 9's fix round):
  - *Concern 1, anthrex's own reply on GitHub.* `GhHost::reply` first reads the logged-in user (`gh api user`, a new allow-list entry taken bare: no path below it, no flag, no method; the fake answers it with its own login). Only a comment by that login, matched without case, holding the exact marker is the reply already posted. A marker pasted by anyone else no longer stops the reply. If `gh api user` names no login, the reply fails with "gh output changed: login" and nothing is posted. The fixture `rest_user.json` is hand-written in GitHub's `GET /user` shape (only `login` is read), because recording it would contact GitHub. Tests: `reply_skips_when_its_marker_is_there` and `a_marker_pasted_by_someone_else_does_not_stop_the_reply`, in the new `host/tests_reply.rs` (moved from `tests_parse.rs` in a move commit, which had 593 lines).
  - *m5, the residual window.* The engine takes a fresh comment as its own when it holds the exact marker of a reply it sent and has had no answer for. The comment's author is not checked for that one fallback, because the engine has no login to check it against. So a writer who pastes, in that window, the exact marker of the reply in flight (it names the run, the PR, the thread and the fix's merge commit) has that one comment ignored. On GitHub, the reply is still posted, since `GhHost::reply` now takes only the user's own comment.
  - *Concern 2, the journal.* `run/delivery/view_trim.rs` (pure) holds the caps, which moved from `review.rs`. Its `journaled(result)` cuts a `PrViewed` to them, and the driver applies it to every op result before the `done` line is written and the `OpDone` is sent (`driver/effects.rs`, one line). The engine therefore gets the same cut view the journal replays. The cuts:
    - the newest 20 comments of each thread keep text, each cut to 4 000 characters;
    - the view keeps 200 000 characters in all, reviews first, then conversation comments, then threads, newest first in each;
    - every id is kept;
    - only a thread's first comment keeps its diff hunk, since the engine reads no other;
    - a kept body the budget empties reads `…`, never `""`, so a changes-requested review still makes its `r` record;
    - anthrex's marker at the end of a cut body is kept after the cut, so a reply anthrex sent is still known by it.

    `a_viewed_pr_is_journaled_within_run_jsons_caps` builds the largest view (100 threads × 50 comments, plus 100 comments and 100 reviews, of 8 000 two-byte characters each) and checks that the journal line stays under `COMPACT_AFTER_BYTES` (1 MiB). Host ops are not executed until M9.2.12, so no driver test covers the call site yet; M9.2.12's driver test should check the `done` line of a large view.
  - *I1, a non-writer's reply.* A fresh comment's author becomes a candidate, and the thread keeps its state, `counted` and batch until a candidate is known to write. A writer makes it `new`, counted and part of the open batch (as before, a `tasked` or `replied` thread a writer comments in again is `new` again). A non-writer's verdict clears the text of that login's comments in the thread, and `text` becomes the newest remaining one. It lowers nothing. Only a created thread that no author may write in becomes `ignored` (no write access). Tests: `a_non_writers_reply_never_lowers_a_writers_thread` (alice's text kept, mallory's cleared, her batch kept, and the fix brief quotes alice only) and `a_non_writers_reply_never_lowers_a_tasked_thread` (it still reaches `replied`). `a_new_comment_in_an_unresolved_thread_counts` now expects the thread to stay `tasked` until bob is known to write.
  - *I2.* `orch.rs` always runs `review_holds` (`let review = …; let held = held.or(review);`). Test: `an_epic_hold_in_the_same_call_does_not_skip_a_review_fix_hold`.
  - *m1.* An `addresses` ref must name a `new` thread that counts: `addresses_needs_a_thread_that_counts`, with the same refusal text.
  - *m2.* A `new` thread a view shows resolved becomes `ignored` ("a resolved thread"). It leaves its open batch, and a batch left empty is dropped. It is no longer counted as not addressed, and the stage logs `stage <n> (PR #<pr>): thread <key> was resolved` (an invented text). A `tasked` or `replied` thread is left alone. Test: `a_thread_resolved_after_it_counted_leaves_its_batch`. The last step of `a_new_comment_in_an_unresolved_thread_counts` now expects the thread to be `ignored`.
  - *m3.*
    - A reply that fails `FAILURES_BEFORE_ATTENTION` (5) times in a row is dropped with the attention line `PR #<pr>: the reply on thread <pr>:<key> was dropped after <k> failures: <error>` (invented). The count is kept on the reply (`ReplyDue.failures`), and a rate limit or a lost login does not count.
    - The generic `reply keeps failing` line and its count are cleared at the drop, so the stage's next reply starts afresh. The dropped reply's line lasts while the PR is open, or until a later reply on that thread is posted.
    - The cap, `REPLIES_PER_THREAD` (3): `reply_comment` is refused with `thread <pr>:<key> already has 3 replies; it gets more once its reviewer comments again` (invented). Automatic replies count toward the cap and are never refused. The count (`ThreadRecord.replies`) starts over each time the thread counts. I read "per batch" as "since the thread last counted", because a `replied` thread never joins a closed batch, so a batch number cannot reset its count.
    - Tests: `a_reply_that_keeps_failing_is_dropped_and_the_next_one_goes` and `replies_per_thread_are_capped_until_it_counts_again`. The tasked test also checks that the automatic reply counts.
  - *m4.* `PERMISSION_WAIT_SECS` = 600 seconds of engine time after a thread's first waiting candidate was seen (`ThreadRecord.waiting_since`). Past it, the unanswered login does not hold the batch. A later answer still counts the author, in a batch of its own. A thread that already counts and is `new` never holds the batch for a further candidate, since it is in the batch whatever the answer. Test: `a_permission_unknown_past_its_deadline_does_not_hold_the_batch`.
  - New model fields, all `#[serde(default)]`: `ThreadRecord.{waiting_since, replies}` and `ReplyDue.failures`.

### Task M9.2.11

- **What exists now.** `run/engine/delivery/sync.rs` (pure): decision 33's base fetch, the queue of due base syncs (`RunDelivery.base_sync_due`), the `from: 0` `PropagateSpec`'s own emit, called from `start_merge` (spec-defect note 7), and every `from: 0` result: merged, already held, conflict (a `FixOf::Base` sync task through `add_fix`), red, lost. `run/engine/delivery/land.rs` (pure): decision 35's landing (merged, closed, reopened), the pause of the stages above a closed PR, the re-plan skip, decision 43's review wait, decision 20's retarget, `delete_merged_branches`, and decision 44's `stage` history line (also at `run cancel`). The pass order is open, CI, review, land, sync, watch.
- **Move commit first.** `engine/propagate.rs` had 592 lines. The sync task's helpers (`hand_back_*`, `lost_merge`, `record_hand_back`, `start_of`, `sync_due`, `sync_merged`) moved to `engine/propagate_sync.rs`, a `#[path]` child module re-exported by `propagate.rs` (`5ce4f2d6`). Lib `#[test]` count: 2432 before, 2432 after.
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **R-4, the parent count, needed host changes.** `FetchReq.parents_of: Option<String>` and `FetchOutcome::Fetched { sha, parents: Option<u32> }` (both `#[serde(default)]`). With `parents_of` set, `GhGit::fetch` runs `git rev-list --parents -n 1 <oid>` after the fetch: a new allow-list arm, read-only, the oid checked by `is_object_id`. A failure, or an answer whose first word is not the oid, gives `None`. `HostOp::Fetch` gains `#[serde(default)] parents_of`. The engine sets it on the base fetch to the merge commit of the first merged PR whose `merge_method` is unknown. **For M9.2.12:** map `HostOp::Fetch.parents_of` to `FetchReq.parents_of`.
  2. **Model fields** (each `#[serde(default)]`, not in Interfaces): `RunDelivery.{base_fetch_due, base_fetch_retry_at}`; `StageDelivery.{landed, sync_red, wait_from}`; `PrRecord.opened_base` (the base the PR was opened against, so a retarget is judged against what anthrex set, never the host's `base`).
  3. **`stage_paused` is derived.** It is now "a lower stage's PR is closed" (`watch.rs`), not `paused_by`, which lagged one step behind the requeue. `land::repause` remains the one writer of `paused_by`, for the log lines and the snapshot.
  4. **When the base is fetched.** It is fetched for the lowest open PR on every view for `always`, and only on a `Conflicting` view for `on_conflict`. It is also fetched after any stage merges. A failed fetch is retried one poll interval later. A base branch gone from the remote is logged (`the base branch <b> is gone from the remote`) and syncs nothing.
  5. **Which stage is synced.** Only the lowest live stage is synced (`sync::target`): not skipped, its PR (if any) neither merged nor closed. If a closed PR below pauses that stage, nothing is synced. A due entry for any other stage is dropped with a log line. A sync waits while the stage is busy, has an open sync task, or has no head. A sha that is already held, in flight, or red on the same stage head is not queued again.
  6. **A red base sync** (tier 2 or the check) is the attention line `the merge of <b>@<sha7> into stage <n> is red: <tests | the check failed>` and the wake note `<line>; plan a fix in stage <n>`. Both are invented. It is not retried on that base and that head (`StageDelivery.sync_red`). A clean sync clears it.
  7. **A base conflict.** `add_fix` with origin `sync` and `FixOf::Base`, owning the conflicted files exactly (a path that cannot be owned exactly refuses the task, with a log line), size M, test mode `check` (`a merge resolution; the tiers check it`). It carries a `SyncState` whose `to_head` is the expected stage head. Its fix holds go through `review_fix::hold_outside`. Its title is `Sync stage <n> with <base>`. Its brief is Interfaces' sync template plus the stage's PR line. The wake note reads `stage <n> conflicts with <b>@<sha7>: sync task <id> added` (invented).
  8. **The `FixOf::Base` arms** (the controller's note):
     - `propagate_sync::lost_merge`: `task_done rejected: sync task must keep the merge of <b>@<sha7>: its head does not contain <sha7>`.
     - `contract_tiers::sync_review_line`: `This is a sync task: judge only how the conflicts were resolved; the changes <b>@<sha7> brought in are not this task's.`
     - `propagate_sync::sync_merged` sends a merged base sync task to `delivery::sync::task_merged`, which records the base as synced.
  9. **R-7.** A lost `from: 0` propagate goes back to `base_sync_due` (`propagate.rs::lost`, one arm), never to `propagate_due`. `propagate.rs::done` routes `from: 0` to `delivery::sync::done` after its tier records. `propagate::start` also skips a stage with a due base sync. That skip is defence in depth: `start_merge` asks `sync::start` first, which drains or emits every due entry under the same conditions, so mutant M5 (removing the skip) is equivalent.
  10. **Bisect.** A `StageMerge::Propagate { from: 0 }` culprit now reads `the first red merge is the merge of the base branch`, not "the propagate of stage 0" (`bisect.rs`, one arm).
  11. **Landing.**
      - *The merged log line.* A merged PR logs `stage <n> (PR #<k>): merged on the host at <commit7>`.
      - *The wake note* is sent when the merge method is known, after the base fetch that counts the parents. It reads `stage <n> PR #<k> was merged (merge commit | squash or rebase | method unknown); stage <m> is being synced and retargeted`, or ends `; that was the last stage`. All of it is invented. "Method unknown" is the reading for a merge with no merge commit, or one whose parents could not be counted. Its history line records `merge_method: none`.
      - *R-11, a held stage.* Landing a held stage, or one whose head is past `pushed_head`, never counts the unpushed head as landed. It logs `…: merged at <pushed7>; <head7> was never pushed, so its commits go up with stage <m>`. At the top of the stack it raises the attention line `stage <n> PR #<k> was merged at <pushed7>, without <head7> that anthrex never pushed; that work is not delivered (anthrex run cancel gives up)`, and the run does not complete while that line stands (`land::settled`).
  12. **Closed.**
      - *Lines.* The PR is the attention line and the wake note `stage <n> PR closed without merging; resume, re-plan, or cancel the rest` (TT's text).
      - *Fix tasks.* Its unfinished fix tasks are cancelled (`cancelled: stage <n> PR closed`). A running task finishes, and a stage above starts no pending task.
      - *Reopened.* A reopened PR clears `landed`, the alert and `history_written` (so its next landing writes its line), and logs `…: reopened; the stages above resume`.
  13. **The re-plan ruling.**
      - *Skip.* A paused stage with no PR whose tasks are all cancelled is skipped: `stage <n>: skipped (its tasks were cancelled while it was paused)`.
      - *Unskip.* A task added to a skipped stage unskips it: `stage <n>: a task was added, so it is no longer skipped`.
      - *The push above waits.* A stage with a PR then holds its pushes while any lower stage still delivers without a PR (`land::lower_unopened`, in the push condition beside `lower_held`). Otherwise that stage's commits would ride in the upper PR. Test: `an_unopened_lower_stage_holds_the_push_above`, added in `delivery_watch_fixes.rs` after mutant M6 survived.
  14. **Retarget.** An open PR is retargeted to `wanted`: the nearest lower open stage's branch, else the base branch. It is judged against `retargeted_to`, else `opened_base`, never `PrRecord.base`. So it also follows a lower stage that merged, closed or opened later, not only the first merge. It waits for quiet: no base fetch or base sync due or in flight, the merge queue idle, no open sync task, the head pushed, no adoption, and the stage neither paused nor held. That gives the order Fetch, base sync, Push, Retarget.
  15. **Delete.** `delete_merged_branches` deletes a merged stage's remote branch once no open PR is based on it, judged by the same `based_on` rule.
  16. **Review wait** (decision 43). It accrues between passes while the PR is open, not paused, has no unfinished fix task of its stage, no pending check, and no CI record outside `tasked`/`to_user`. The interval since the last pass counts when the PR was waiting at its start.
  17. **History.** The `stage` line is written once, when the merge method is known, or at close, or at `run cancel` for an open PR (`open_at_cancel`).
      - `time_to_open_secs` is `opened_at − ready_at`.
      - `ci_rounds` and `sync_tasks` count the stage's tasks by origin.
      - `history_written` is set even when history is off, so the run can complete.
      - `run/stats.rs` and `history_io.rs` were not modified: their `stage` arms (skip, `record_id`) landed in M9.2.1.
  18. **Completion.** `may_complete` also needs `land::settled`: no base fetch due, no unlanded alert, and every landed stage's line written.
  19. **Older tests changed:**
      - `delivery_requests.rs`'s `land` helper answers the base fetch and the retarget.
      - `delivery_watch_adopt.rs`'s `a_paused_stage_starts_no_task` pauses with a closed PR record and resumes by reopening it.
      - `opening_waits…` answers the base sync with `AlreadyHeld`.
      - The literals of `host/tests*.rs` and `run/delivery/tests.rs` gained the new fields.
  20. **`squash_merged_stage_sync_is_clean_and_changes_no_file`** has two parts:
      - a real-git driver test (`driver/stage_ops_propagate_tests.rs`), since only git can show that the tree does not change;
      - an engine test of the same name in `delivery_land.rs`, for the engine side.

- **Fix round 1** (the controller's rulings on the review; committed on top of the 9.0.7 merge `3a24415`):
  - *I1. Every merged stage gets its merge method.*
    - The problem: `fetched()` cleared `base_fetch_due`, and a fetch counts only one merge commit. A second merge seen around one fetch therefore never got its method or its `stage` line, and the run never completed.
    - Now `sync::method_due` names the oldest merged stage whose method is unknown and whose merge commit is an object id. While one is left, `sync::pass` treats the base fetch as due by itself, one fetch at a time.
    - Tests:
      - `two_merges_seen_before_one_base_fetch_each_get_a_method`: the first fetch fails and stage 2 merges during the wait.
      - `a_merge_seen_while_the_base_fetch_is_in_flight_gets_its_method`.
    - Each asserts both `stage` lines and that the run completes (new file `tests/delivery_land_fixes.rs`).
  - *I2. A PR reopened and merged between two polls lands as merged.*
    - `landing`'s `(Merged, Some(Closed))` arm runs `reopened` (clearing the `<n>/closed` alert and `history_written`, and logging the reopen) and then `merged`.
    - Test: `a_pr_reopened_and_merged_between_polls_lands_as_merged`. The closed line goes, and the history's last line for the stage is `merged`.
  - *m1. A base being resolved is not queued again.* `sync::queue` skips a sha that an unfinished `FixOf::Base { base_sha }` task of the stage carries (`resolving`).
    - Test: `a_base_its_sync_task_carries_is_not_queued_again`. It checks for one "moved" line and no base sync after the task merges.
  - *m2. A non-object-id merge commit fails nothing.*
    - The engine asks git only about a merge commit that passes `allow::is_object_id`. Any other is `merge_method: none` at once (`land::merged`), like a missing one.
    - `GhHost::parents` returns `Ok(None)` for such an oid before calling git.
    - Tests:
      - `a_merge_commit_that_is_not_an_object_id_asks_git_nothing` (engine: the fetch carries no `parents_of`, and the run completes);
      - `a_base_fetch_counts_the_merge_commits_parents` (real git: `not-an-object-id` and `HEAD` answer `parents: None`).
  - *m3.* `host/tests_git.rs::the_rev_list_arm_takes_only_a_full_object_id_read_only`. The one accepted shape is checked, and these are refused:
    - a ref name;
    - a branch name;
    - `-n 2`;
    - `--all`, in either position;
    - an abbreviated sha;
    - an extra oid;
    - a trailing pathspec;
    - the write context;
    - no hook flags.

    It lives in `tests_git.rs` because `tests_allow.rs` has 583 lines. It pins the existing arm, so it passed before the round.
  - *m4. Ruling 97, tested directly.* `a_base_the_user_set_is_left_alone_until_a_stage_below_changes`:
    - a view reports stage 2's base changed by hand to `user-picked`;
    - nothing is retargeted while nothing below changes;
    - stage 1's merge then puts it back on `main`, once, after the push.

    It pins existing behaviour, so it passed before the round.
  - *m5. A reading.* A merged stage's unfinished fix tasks are not cancelled (`land::merged` leaves them; only `closed` cancels).
    - Below a delivering stage, their commits go up with the next stage.
    - On the top stage, they are never pushed and raise no line.
    - Filed for M9.5 in the follow-ups plan.
  - *m6. Open questions, unchanged:*
    - `waiting()` counts a halted or paused run's gap between two passes as review time, if the PR was waiting at the first of them.
    - `cancelled()` writes no line for a stage that merged but whose method is not known yet at the cancel. With I1's fix that window is one base fetch.
  - *RED.* Before the fix, the five new engine tests that target a change failed:
    - I1 (a) and (b), each at the second base fetch;
    - I2, at the closed line;
    - m1, at the "moved" count;
    - m2 engine, at the fetch's `parents_of`.

    The extended host test failed with `Forbidden(... rev-list --parents -n 1 not-an-object-id)` against the old `GhHost::parents`. m3 and m4 pin existing behaviour.


### Task M9.2.12

- **What exists now.** `host/select.rs` (decision 15: `CodeHostChoice { Gh { bin }, Fake { dir } }`, `CodeHostChoice::from_vars`, `build`; a `fake` choice with no directory builds a host whose every call fails with `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=fake needs ANTHREX_FAKE_HOST_DIR`). `ManagerConfig.code_host`, read once in `from_vars` beside `ANTHREX_CLAUDE_BIN`; `for_tests` pins `Gh { bin: /nonexistent/anthrex-test/gh }` (`manager::TEST_GH_BIN`). `RunContext.host` (built once, in `RunContext::new`, from the manager's choice; `with_host` for tests) and `RunService::host()`, which `profile::service::wire` hands the profile service (`ProfileService::set_host`). `run/driver/host_ops.rs` (decision 8's executor), `run/driver/delivery.rs` (decision 3's resolution, decision 17's preflight, the remote seal, `run deliver`/`run watch`), `profile/delivery.rs` (decision 3's detection). `driver/ops.rs`'s `Host` arm calls the executor; `requests.rs` routes `Start.delivery`, `StartGoal.delivery`, `Deliver` and `Watch`; `build.rs`'s `build_plan` became `build_delivered` (one extra argument); `adapt_goal.rs` preflights before triage.
- **The carried rulings, each with its test.**
  - *Every `CodeHost` call on `spawn_blocking`, off both locks* (`host_calls_run_off_the_engine_lock`): the executor runs every call on a blocking thread (directly, or inside `GitQueue::write`, which is itself `spawn_blocking`); the engine lock is taken once, before the call, only to read the run's seal. `grep -n "spawn_blocking\|queue.write" host_ops.rs` shows both paths; `grep -rn "daemon::lock\|crate::lock(&self.manager" host_ops.rs` shows none.
  - *Push, fetch and delete through `GitQueue::write`, the adoption's compare-and-swap inside the queue* (`push_and_fetch_go_through_the_git_queue`): `GhHost::fetch` runs the swap, and the whole `fetch` call is the queued closure.
  - *The remote's URLs recorded at start, a change refused* (`a_changed_remote_refuses_push_and_fetch`), see deviation 2.
  - *A `JoinError` (a `FakeGh` panic) is never retryable* (`a_panicking_host_is_a_halt_never_a_retry`), see deviation 3.
  - *The mode resolved once, at start*: `run start --plan` resolves and preflights in `build_delivered` (after M8a's git preflight and the profile choice, before `build_run`); `run start --goal` in `start_goal` before triage, handing the result (`DeliveryStart::Done`) to the fast build and to `start_planned`, which never resolve again. `preflight_runs_before_triage_for_a_goal` counts one `gh auth status` per start.
  - *`HostOp::Reply.marker` into `ReplyReq.marker`*, *`HostOp::Fetch.parents_of` into `FetchReq.parents_of`* (`every_op_reaches_its_request`, which checks every op's fields and answer).
  - *The viewer login cached once per host* (`host/tests_reply.rs::the_viewer_login_is_read_once_per_host`): `GhHost.viewer`, keyed by the GitHub host, filled on the first successful `gh api user`; a failed read is not cached. The cache lives as long as the host, which the daemon builds once: it is read once per daemon and GitHub host (`the_viewer_login_is_cached_per_host`, fix round 1, m8). A known limit: after a `gh auth switch` while the daemon runs, replies are matched against the old login until the daemon restarts, so a re-issued reply could be posted twice.
  - *The driver's `view_trim` call* (`a_large_view_is_trimmed_before_the_journal`): a 100-comment, 100×50-thread view of 8 000-character bodies goes through `execute`; its `done` line is under `COMPACT_AFTER_BYTES` and every comment is cut to `COMMENT_KEPT_CHARS`.
  - `contract.rs` and `merge.rs` are untouched.
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **`CodeHost::detect(root, remote)`** is added to the trait (with `GhHost`, `FakeHost` and the unconfigured host): decision 3's detection runs preflight's first checks only (the remote's URL, `gh --version`, `gh auth status`, `gh repo view`) and must not dry-run a push, which `preflight` always does. `gh_preflight.rs` splits those checks into `check_repo`, which `check_preflight` calls first; its texts are unchanged.
  2. **The seal is the effective URLs' digest.** The ruling says `url`/`pushurl`. `RunDelivery.remote_seal` (`#[serde(default)]`) is a SHA-256 of `git remote get-url --all <remote>` and `get-url --push --all`, which also covers an `insteadOf`/`pushInsteadOf` rewrite a worker could add to the shared `.git/config`. Since fix round 1 (I1) both reads are `CodeHost::remote_seal`, run through `GhHost::git`, so they pass decision 7's allow-list: two exact arms in the read context, for the run's remote only (`host/tests_seal.rs`). A digest, not the URLs: a URL may carry a token, and `run.json` would keep it. It is read at preflight and, inside the queued closure, before every `Push`, `Fetch` and `DeleteBranch`; a change answers (fix round 1, m5) `HostError::Forbidden("<op> on remote <r>, whose URLs changed since the run started; anthrex pushes and fetches only where preflight checked (restore remote.<r>.url, remote.<r>.pushurl and any url.<base>.insteadOf or pushInsteadOf rule, then run anthrex run resume)")` (invented), which halts the run with decision 11's `anthrex refused its own host command: …`. `gh` calls are not checked: they name `--repo <owner>/<name>` and never read the remote. A run with no seal (built before this task, so never `pr`) is not checked.
  3. **A panicking host call is a halt.** The call runs under `catch_unwind` inside its blocking closure (so the queue path and the direct path alike), and a panic answers `HostError::Forbidden("<op>, which panicked: <message>")` with a `tracing::error!` (fix round 1, m5: it reads right after the engine's `anthrex refused its own host command: `). A blocking task that was cancelled (a shutdown), or a queue that lost its task, is `HostError::Failed`, retried when next due (fix round 1, m4: only `JoinError::is_panic()` is a halt). The engine halts on `Forbidden` (decision 11) and never re-issues it; `run resume` re-derives the op, which panics again.
  4. **The executor's own bound.** Decision 9's bounds are per command, enforced by the runner. Each op also gets the sum of its commands' bounds plus 5 s (`host_ops::bound`: a view 65 s, a push 185 s with the seal's two reads), so a host that ignores its timeouts still answers `TimedOut("<op> did not answer within <n> s")` (invented), retried when next due. A queued op's bound includes its wait for the queue; the queue's own write finishes unobserved (it is cancellation-safe). `RunContext.host_cap` is the test seam that shortens it.
  5. **Preflight's whole bound** is `PREFLIGHT_BOUND` (335 s: the checks' own, the seal's reads, 5 s), with the invented text `preflight: gh did not answer within <n> s`; each check's own timeout keeps the Messages text (`gh --version: gh did not answer within 30 s`). A seal that cannot be read refuses the start: `cannot read remote <r>'s URLs: <git's error>` (invented).
  6. **Detection for every proposal but an edit** (corrected in fix round 1, m2). `verify_phase` sets `profile.delivery` from detection for every origin except `Edit`: `Detect`, `Goal` and `Auto { stale }` alike, so a stale profile's re-detection replaces the table, as an automatic re-detection replaces every key; a `profile edit` re-verification (`ProposalOrigin::Edit`) keeps the stored table, so editing `check` never flips a user's `delivery.mode = local` back to `pr`. A host-less service (a test's) proposes no table; detection's bound is 125 s.
  7. **The unknown `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST`** warning reads `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=<value> is not gh or fake; using gh` (invented), logged once at daemon start. An empty variable is unset.
  8. **`lifecycle.rs` is unchanged.** It already builds the daemon's `RunContext` once (`RunContext::from_config`), which builds the host once from `ManagerConfig.code_host`; the profile service gets the same `Arc` through `wire`.
  9. **Files beyond the list:** `host/{mod.rs, gh.rs, gh_preflight.rs, fake/mod.rs}` (deviation 1 and the viewer cache), `manager/mod.rs` (re-export `TEST_GH_BIN`), `run/delivery/mod.rs` (`remote_seal`), `run/driver.rs` (+2, the two `mod`s; 591), `run/driver/adapt.rs` (`ProfileChoice.delivery`, the stored profile's table, and its test literal), `profile/{mod.rs, service.rs}` (`set_host`). Tests: `host/tests_select.rs`, `host/tests_reply.rs` (+1), `run/driver/{host_ops_tests.rs, host_ops_tests_fields.rs, delivery_tests.rs, delivery_tests_start.rs}`, `profile/tests_detect.rs`; `adapt_goal_tests.rs` (one call's arguments). `build_plan` is kept for the tests as a test-only wrapper of `build_delivered` (`driver/delivery.rs`).
  10. **The log tail.** `failed_logs_are_written_0600_and_kept_out_of_the_journal`: the log file (≤ `max_bytes`, 0600, in a 0700 `<data_dir>/delivery`) is never journaled; the `done` line still carries `LogFile.tail` (≤ 48 KiB), task 9's accepted design, and the test bounds the line at `CI_SUMMARY_INPUT_BYTES` + 8 KiB.
  11. **Preflight 5's exact text** (the controller's ruling on concern 7, fix round 1): anthrex's own sentence naming the failure, then git's last stderr line quoted, on one line (`one_line`) and capped at 300 characters (`last_line`): `a dry-run push to <remote> was refused, so anthrex cannot push there; git said: "<line>"`. For a remote with no repository behind it the line is `and the repository exists.` (git 2.50.1, `LC_ALL=C`).
  12. **A `pr` run starts watching** (`watching = true`, `poll_base_secs = poll_secs`); a `local` run keeps both off and no repository or seal.
  13. **Task 10's argv fixture follows the viewer cache.** `host/tests.rs::every_method` scripted one `gh api user` per reply; with the cache the second reply reads none, so the fixture drops that answer and that expected call (27 calls become 26, the conversation reply's index 23 becomes 22). No other change to a test from an earlier task.
  14. **The start tests answer within a deadline.** `delivery_tests_start.rs` asks every request through `ask` (60 s), so a regression that lets a refused start reach the unspawned engine fails instead of hanging (the first run of mutant M10 hung that way).
- **Evidence.** The code was drafted before its tests, so RED is shown by 18 mutants, each applied alone, its tests run, the file restored from a copy. Every one is RED: M1 the executor arm unwired (3 failed); M2 writes past the queue; M3 no bound; M4 a panic answered `Failed` (retryable); M5 `marker` dropped; M6 `parents_of` dropped; M7 the seal unchecked; M8 the seal from `remote.origin.url` alone (blind to `insteadOf`); M9 the goal start resolving no mode; M10 `--plan` resolving no mode (2 failed); M11 the viewer not cached; M12 a failed detection still proposing `pr`; M13 the view not trimmed; M14 the delivery directory 0755; M15 a `pr` run not watching; M16 an unknown `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST` chosen as `fake`; M17 the default `pr`; M18 the log written into the data root. GREEN: `cargo test -p anthrex-daemon --lib`, 2481 passed; clippy 1.99 (host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`) and `cargo +1.98 check` clean.

Grown: `run/driver.rs` 591, `ops.rs` 541, `build.rs` 525 (+12 since M9.2.1's 513, against +8: the `DeliveryStart` argument and its doc), `adapt.rs` 531, `adapt_goal.rs` 428, `requests.rs` 430, `context.rs` 162, `profile/service.rs` 503, `service_run.rs` 448 (+5 against +4), `host/gh.rs` 486, `host/gh_preflight.rs` 217. `run/contract.rs` (595) and `run/engine/merge.rs` (583) are untouched.

#### Fix round 1 (task M9.2.12)

- **I1, the seal through the allow-list.** `CodeHost::remote_seal(root, remote)` (a new trait method; `GhHost`, `FakeHost`, the unconfigured host and the test stub), implemented in `gh_preflight.rs` as two `GhHost::git` reads and their digest. `allow.rs` accepts exactly `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null remote get-url --all <remote>` and `… remote get-url --push --all <remote>`, before the blanket refusal of `--all` (which still refuses `fetch --all` and `push --all`), and only with the read flags and the context's remote, never one starting with `-`. `the_seal_reads_pass_the_allow_list_exactly` refuses a missing `--all`, the flags reordered, an extra argument, a second `--all`, `-v`, another remote, an option-shaped or empty remote, the write flags, no flags, `set-url` and `remote -v`. `driver/delivery.rs` lost its own `seal` and `HostExec` its `git` field; preflight and `unchanged` call the host. A failed read answers `HostError::Failed("cannot read remote <r>'s URLs: <git's last line>")`, never a URL.
- **I2, `run start`'s reply bound.** `PREFLIGHT_BOUND` moved to `host/mod.rs` and is public. The CLI's new `RUN_START_TIMEOUT` = `RUN_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` (M8a's git preflight) + `daemon::host::PREFLIGHT_BOUND` + 30 s = 545 s, for `RunRequest::Start`; `GOAL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` is now derived from it (+ 600 s + 30 s = 1175 s), because a `pr` goal preflights before triage (beyond the ruling's letter, the same bound for the same step). `run_start_outwaits_both_preflights` pins both. The smoke scripts follow (`RUN_CMD_TIMEOUT` 600 s, `GOAL_CMD_TIMEOUT` 1300 s), and `docs/timing-budgets.md` gains a "Recorded, from M9.2.12" section and updated rows 127, 166, 241, 249, 284 and 337. Profile requests keep 180 s: detection runs in the background job, after the reply.
- **m1, `gh` pinned in the harnesses.** `pin_agents` (`crates/cli/tests/support/mod.rs`), `RunHarness`'s environment (`support/run_harness.rs`), `context_with` (`support/decider.rs`), `mcp_cli.rs` and `scripts/pty-smoke.py` set `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=gh` and `ANTHREX_GH_BIN=/nonexistent/anthrex-test/gh`. `test_rigs_pin_agents.rs` now requires `ANTHREX_GH_BIN` of every file that starts `anthrex` (it found `mcp_cli.rs`); a `from_vars` map still needs only the two runtimes.
- **m3**: the decider stand-in is written under another name, closed, renamed into place and executed once (a guard line exits) before the test starts, retrying on `ExecutableFileBusy` within 10 s.
- **m6**: the edit in `host_calls_run_off_the_engine_lock` must be the engine's own `Done("run edit", "applied 0 edits")`. **m7**: `deliver_and_watch_are_routed_to_the_engine` (each label, and `run deliver`'s own running-state refusal). **m9**: timing-budgets rows for the new test bounds. **m11**: `private_dir` makes only the `delivery` directory 0700 (parents get the default mode) and tightens one that exists (`only_the_delivery_directory_is_private`); the stray `use` moved to the top.
- **Evidence.** Nine mutants, one per fix, are RED (task-12-report.md, "Fix round 1"). `cargo test -p anthrex-daemon --lib`: 2497 passed; the CLI binary's unit tests (58), `test_rigs_pin_agents`, `mcp_cli`, `decider_call` and `run_e2e_adapt` pass.

### Task M9.2.13

- **What exists now.** `run/delivery/digest.rs`: `block(run, Shape)` builds decision 29's `delivery` key with Interfaces' exact keys (`{"mode": "local"}` in local mode), `Shape::{FULL, FINGERPRINT, TRIMMED, NO_COMMENTS}`, and the trim steps `trim`/`drop_comments`. `orch/digest.rs::build` gains the one key (the fingerprint builds it without `comment` and with every thread). `run/delivery/contract.rs::DELIVERY_RULES` (exact, rules 38–42) is a `macro_rules!` literal so `orch/contract.rs` can `concat!` it onto `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT` (a `const` cannot be an argument of `concat!`). `get_context`'s `limits` gains `stage_target_lines` (`[low, high]`, the run's frozen value). `mcp::tools_orch::plan_edit` gains `reply_comment` (last in `op`) with `pr` (integer ≥ 1), `thread` (1–64 characters) and `body` (1–4000), and `plan_task` gains `addresses` (at most 20 strings of 1–64 characters), the same bounds as `orch/tools_bounds.rs` (task M9.2.10) except `body`, whose 1–4000 is `reply_edit`'s rule.
- **The ruling "the digest quotes only writers' comments".** `RunDelivery::writes(login)` is the one rule (listed reviewer, else `owner_ok`, else the cached permission); `engine/delivery/review.rs::writes` now calls it (−13 lines there). A thread shows a `comment` only when it counted and is `new` or in the stage's open batch, and the comment holds only the comments whose author `writes` is `Some(true)`: a comment whose author's permission is still being asked is not quoted (`delivery_digest.rs::digest_quotes_only_writers_comments_of_a_real_view`, through the engine's real intake).
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **A thread's quote within 2000 characters stays closed.** Cutting the quoted block itself would cut its closing fence off. `quote::comment_within(login, text, max)` cuts the comment's text so the label, the fence and `(cut; the whole comment is on the pull request)` (invented) fit. A review thread with several writers' comments shows each quoted, oldest first, the newest kept when they do not all fit.
  2. **The trim order.** Before anything else in the digest: comments cut to 300, then each stage's threads to 10, then the stages whose PR merged or closed dropped (the brief's "then resolved stages"); each step rebuilds the block from the run. Beyond the brief: the comments are removed (`null`) just before the digest's general 120-character string cut, which would otherwise cut a quote's fence.
  3. **Which threads are shown.** The `new` and open-batch threads first, then the others newest first, each group in the PR's order; at most 20 per stage. `fix_tasks` lists each origin's ids in plan order, the newest 20 (a bound the Interfaces do not give). A stage with no PR shows `pr`, `url`, `state`, `ci` and `ci_line` as `null` and no threads.
  4. **`ci_line`** (the Interfaces give one example): for the newest CI record of the PR's pushed head, `<category> failed at <sha7>: <doing>`, the category `CI` when it is unknown or not yet known, and `<doing>` one of `reading the failed logs`, `summarising the log`, `re-running the failed jobs`, `reproducing it locally`, `bisecting the stage`, `fix task <id> <state>`, `handed to the user` (invented); `null` when the head has no red record.
  5. **Rule 34's stage size** now reads `Aim for stage_target_lines changed lines per stage, the range get_context gives under limits (300 to 800 unless the user set another);` (invented; the contract must not vary, so it names the key rather than the value). `PLANNER_CONTRACT` has no stage-size sentence and is unchanged. Rule 28's "A message or refresh is always the only edit in its edit_plan call" is unchanged; rule 40 says it for `reply_comment`, and `ONE_EDIT_RULE` was already extended by task M9.2.10.
  6. **Pinning tests changed, not loosened:** `orch/contract_tests.rs::ORCHESTRATOR_EXPECTED` (rule 34's sentence, rules 38–42 appended; this is the brief's `contract_tests_still_pass`, the M9 family kept exact), `orch/digest_fixture.json` (+`"delivery": {"mode": "local"}`), `mcp/src/fixtures/orch_tools.json` (both `plan_edit` copies: `reply_comment`, `pr`, `thread`, `body`; all four `plan_task` copies: `addresses`).
  7. **Files beyond the list:** `run/delivery/{mod.rs (writes, +18), quote.rs (comment_within, +30), snapshot.rs (ci_state pub(crate))}`, `run/orch/digest_trim.rs` (the two calls, +9; 211), `engine/delivery/review.rs` (−13; 570), and `run/engine/tests/delivery_digest.rs` (the fingerprint against real polls, and the writer filter through a real view), registered in `engine/tests/mod.rs`. The brief's `fingerprint_changes_when_a_thread_arrives_but_not_on_a_poll_with_no_change` is there; `orch/tests_delivery.rs` adds `fingerprint_ignores_comment_text_but_not_threads`.
- **Line counts.** New: `run/delivery/digest.rs` 280, `run/delivery/contract.rs` 21, `orch/tests_delivery.rs` 428, `engine/tests/delivery_digest.rs` 87. Grown: `orch/contract.rs` 489 (≤ 600), `orch/digest.rs` 394 (+6: the key, the import, the test module), `orch/context.rs` 413, `mcp/src/tools_orch.rs` 277 (+6), `run/delivery/mod.rs` 544. `run/contract.rs` (595) and `run/engine/merge.rs` (583) are untouched.

#### Fix round 1 (task M9.2.13)

- **I1, the cap.** `Shape::LAST` adds five delivery steps, taken in `digest_trim.rs` after every other step and before any unfinished task is dropped:
  1. only the threads to decide on (counted and new or batched), 10 a stage;
  2. then 3 a stage;
  3. then every string of the block cut to 40 characters;
  4. then 3 fix task ids per origin;
  5. last, each stage as `{stage, pr, state, ci, threads_omitted}`, where `threads_omitted` (invented) counts the stage's listed threads.

  The last form is a few numbers per stage, at most `STAGES_MAX` stages, so the cap holds whatever the delivery holds. `the_cap_holds_whatever_the_delivery_holds` pins it: 32 open stages, 20 threads each on 250-character paths by a 39-character login, and 64 fix tasks, with `size <= DIGEST_MAX_BYTES` and `omitted_tasks == 0`.
- **I2.** A `new` thread that has not counted is not listed until it counts, so the fingerprint moves when it does. The first group is `current(s, t)`. Ignored threads stay listed, never with text.
- **m1.** `drop_comments` is pinned by a block still over the cap after decision 29's three steps.
- **m2.** `reply_edit::apply` refuses a thread that never counted, after `unknown thread`: `thread <pr>:<key> never counted: anthrex replies only on a thread a writer or a listed reviewer wrote in` (invented).
- **m3.** `ci_line` is shown only while `ci` is `red`.
- **m4.** `ONE_EDIT_RULE`'s doc comment names `reply_comment`.
- **m5.** The thread cut is pinned alone, with resolved stages still shown.
- **Files.**
  - Changed: `run/delivery/digest.rs` (372), `run/orch/digest_trim.rs` (216, +5), `run/delivery/reply_edit.rs` (151, task 10's file, as the ruling asks).
  - New test file: `run/orch/tests_delivery_trim.rs` (114), declared from `tests_delivery.rs` (511), so `orch/digest.rs` stays at 394.

### Task M9.2.14

- **What exists now.** `crates/cli/src/run_cmd/delivery.rs` (409): `--delivery`'s check (`mode`), `run prs` (`prs_table`, `--json`), `run deliver`, `run watch`, `run status`'s `delivery:` and `prs:` lines (`status_lines`) and its `running (delivering)` state (`delivering`), and the hidden `run fake-github --dir <dir> <verb>` with the eleven verbs of Interfaces "`FakeGithubCtl`". `run_cmd.rs` (511, +18) takes the flag and four variants (`Prs`, `Deliver`, `Watch` as `Args` structs from `delivery.rs`, and the hidden `FakeGithub`); `status.rs` (348, +3) one state arm and one call; `finish.rs` (190, +5) forwards a `pr` run's `run accept` to the daemon. `cli/src/main.rs` is untouched (600).
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **`--delivery`'s exit code.** clap's own parse error exits 2 (`main.rs`'s `Cli::parse()`, which this task may not touch), and the Interfaces say "anything else exits 1 with clap's message". So clap takes the flag as text (`value_name = "pr|local"`), and `delivery::mode` re-checks it with clap's own `ValueEnum` parser on a one-argument command, before the daemon is asked anything: clap's message (`invalid value 'github' for '--delivery <pr|local>'` … `[possible values: pr, local]`), exit 1. An empty value gets clap's `a value is required for '--delivery <pr|local>'`. `run start --goal` takes the flag too (`adapt.rs`, one tuple argument: not in the brief's file list, but `RunRequest::StartGoal.delivery` is set there).
  2. **`run prs`'s readings.** One line per `RunInfo.stages` entry, `<n>/<N>`. `CI` is `none`, `pending`, `green` or `red` (the Interfaces show only `red`); a stage with no PR shows `–` in every column but `STATE`. `THREADS` leaves out `ignored`. `FIX TASKS` is every entry of `StagePrInfo.fix_tasks` (in flight first) as `<id> <state>` (the origin word dropped, as the example shows `fix3 working`), comma-separated, `–` when none. `paused` only for a PR whose `paused` is set: the snapshot has no pause flag for a stage without a PR (decision 37 opens no PR above a closed one), so such a stage reads `waiting`. Cells pad as `run status`'s do (a cell as wide as its column or wider ends in one space). `--json` prints one entry per stage, `null` where it has no PR, so entry `n - 1` is stage `n` (what `PrRig::wait_stage` reads); a local run's `--json` prints `[]` (its text form prints the local line); both exit 0.
  3. **`run status`'s `prs:` line** lists every stage PR once one exists: `#<n> <state>` for a merged or closed one, `#<n> <open|paused> (ci <state>[, <k> thread(s)])` otherwise, where `<k>` is decision 42's count (`new` + `tasked`) and is left out at 0. The `delivery:` line follows the orchestrator's lines. `running (delivering)` replaces the state only when the run is `running` and `DeliveryInfo.delivering`.
  4. **`run accept` in `pr` mode** goes to the daemon whatever the run's state, so decision 38's text is printed (the CLI's own "accept applies only to a complete run" check is for local runs). `run discard` already printed the daemon's refusal.
  5. **`run fake-github`.** `--dir` is required (clap, exit 2) and must be an existing directory (`fake-github: <dir> is not a directory`, exit 1, nothing created; invented), so a mistyped path never makes a fresh fake. Verbs: `create-repo <owner> <name> <bare> [--default-branch main]`, `log-in <host>`, `set-permission <user> <admin|maintain|write|triage|read|none>`, `set-ci <rules JSON>` (an array of `CiRule`), `comment <pr> <user> <body>` and `review-comment <pr> <user> <path> <line> <body>` (print the id), `merge <pr> --method merge|squash|rebase [--delete-branch]`, `close <pr>`, `commit <branch> <path> <content> <message>` (prints the sha), `prs` and `forbidden` (JSON). It calls `FakeGithubCtl` only: no daemon, no `gh`, no network; a `FakeGithubCtl` panic becomes `fake-github: <message>`, exit 1.
  6. **`PrRig` is added here** (`crates/cli/tests/support/run_pr.rs`, 169), since this task's tests are the first to need a PR (the brief's "Shared test helpers": added in the task that first needs them). It is the brief's helper, with two readings: `wait_stage` takes the `&RunHarness` as its first argument (a rig that borrowed the harness would block `restart_daemon`), and `pr_harness(extra_toml)` builds an unstarted harness, the rig and the brief's `[delivery]` test configuration, then starts the daemon on the fake, so no test needs a restart to switch hosts. Both `insteadOf` and `pushInsteadOf` are set (as `driver/delivery_tests.rs`'s rig does). `Drop` asserts `forbidden.jsonl` is empty unless the test is already panicking.
- **Evidence.** RED: the nine end-to-end tests against the binary before the change (all failed: `unexpected argument '--delivery'`, `unrecognized subcommand 'prs' / 'watch' / 'fake-github'`); the five unit tests did not compile. Four mutants, each RED: the `running (delivering)` arm removed; the origin word kept in `FIX TASKS`; the `--dir` existence check removed; `finish.rs`'s `pr` forward removed. GREEN: `cargo test -p anthrex --bin anthrex` (63), `--test run_cli_delivery` (9, 9.2 s), `--test run_cli` (12), `anthrex-daemon --test test_rigs_pin_agents` (2); clippy 1.99 on the host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, `cargo +1.98 check`. `docs/timing-budgets.md` gains a "Recorded, from M9.2.14" row for `PrRig`'s waits.

The pinning test `local_run_renders_as_before` pins 9.0.7's and 9.1's render tests, which run unchanged, plus a local run's inspections.
