Test fixtures `tree/tests/pr_fixtures.rs` (three stages: `#141` merged, `#142` open and red with checks, threads and fix tasks, `#143` pending; and a single-stage `#7`), two render-audit fixtures (`run view delivering` on stage 2, `run view delivering one stage` on the root), and `ui/run_pr_tests.rs` (both sizes, ASCII, hostile text, the title).
- **Readings and deviations** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **The row suffix** (decision 42): `  #<n>  ci <mark>` then `  <k> thread(s)` (`new` + `tasked`, left out at 0) and `  paused`; `  #<n>  merged` / `  #<n>  closed`; with no PR, `  skipped` for a stage in `DeliveryInfo.skipped_stages` and `  no PR yet` otherwise; empty in a local run (or with no `delivery`). `run prs`'s table says `waiting` for the last; a graph row says what is missing.
  2. **`MAX_STAGE_NODE_WIDTH` 37 → 62**: 9.0.7's widest stage text (31) plus the PR suffix up to `  #9999  ci ✗  99 threads` (25), plus the glyph and the box. A box takes only what its text needs, so a local run's stage boxes are unchanged (9.0.7's paint tests pass as they were). In the compact list at 80×24 a stage row is cut by width like any row (`… #142  ci ✗  3 …`); the stage panel below it carries every line.
  3. **The stage panel's lines**: `pr` `#142 open, based on <base>, opened 14:02` (`, merged 15:10` once merged, ` · paused`), `url`, `ci` per check of the most recent head `✓ build · ✗ test (fix3)` (a wrapping row, so the checks are `·`-separated: the wrap folds decision 42's two spaces) or, with no checks, the PR's CI as a word (`no checks`, `pending`, `green`, `red`), `threads` `2 open, 1 addressed, 1 replied` (`new`, `tasked`, `replied`; `, 4 ignored` when any; `none` before the first), and `fix tasks` by origin from `StagePrInfo.fix_tasks` (`ci: fix3 working; review: fix4 merged`, a `merge_queue` label read `merge queue`, an entry of another shape shown as it came). With a PR, that line stands for 9.1's `fix tasks` line (the PR's list holds every non-plan task of the stage with its state); without one, 9.1's line is unchanged. Every host or agent text (`base`, `url`, a check's name, its fix task, a fix-task entry, the remote and repo) goes through `run_format::clean`.

The title row's right text is 9.0.7's (`Rows` layout): dropped when the name and it do not fit, so at 80×24 `running (delivering)` shows in neither header; it shows at 120×40.

#### Fix round 1 (task M9.2.15)

The review's findings and ruling R-13's remaining bullets (ruled into this round). The "Not done here" bullet above is superseded: all four bullets are now done.

- **R-13 (a), typed delivery alerts.** `DeliveryInfo.alerts: Vec<DeliveryAlert { kind, stage, text }>`. The kinds are `ci_handed_to_user`, `pr_closed_unmerged`, `gh_logged_out`, `host_op_held` and `review_rounds_over_cap`. The field is named MessagePack, left out when empty, and loads from an old snapshot. `PROTO_VERSION` stays 14, and the version doc says why. Round trips are in `proto/src/delivery_alert_tests.rs`.
  - The daemon builds both lists from one source, `engine/delivery/alerts.rs::lines`. That module also produces `attention` (moved from `watch.rs`) and `alerts`.
  - A line's kind comes from the key it was filed under (`classify`), never from its text. The keys are `auth`, `<n>/ci/…`, `<n>/closed`, `<n>/cap/…` and a failure `<n|run>/<op>`, plus each held stage.
  - Replies, refused fixes, full pages and the review lines (unaddressed threads) stay attention lines only.
  - The text goes through `safe_text::one_line`. There is one assertion per kind beside its attention line in `engine/tests/delivery_*.rs`.
  - TUI: `AlertKey::Delivery { run, kind, stage, n }` at priority 3, in `pr` mode only. `n` is the ordinal within a kind and stage, so two capped threads are two alerts.
  - Enter on `host_op_held` preselects `resume`. The other kinds open on the menu's first entry, because the user acts on GitHub or with `gh`.
  - The Alerts view's detail shows a `phase` row for the kind (`delivery · held`, and so on), plus `reason`, `stage <n> of <m>` and `run`.
- **R-13 (b), the task panel.** OUTCOME, 9.0.7's successor to 9.0.5's STATUS and RESULT, gains two rows after `pipeline` and `check`, in `pr` mode only:
  - `pr`: `#142 open · ci red`, `#141 merged`, `#140 closed`, `skipped` or `no PR yet`. CI is shown as a word, so the row carries no mark to colour.
  - `fixes`: the task's `fixes`, cleaned.
- **R-13 (c), the plan review.** A `pr` run's gate shows `delivered as <n> pull requests to <repo>` (or `1 pull request`) on its own plain row under the summary.
  - `<n>` is the plan's distinct stages. The repo is cleaned and folded, and ` to …` is left out when the repo is blank.
  - A hold's review does not show the row, because its tasks join PRs already planned. `ReviewLayout.delivery` and `stacked`'s new parameter carry it.
- **R-13 (d), the goal dialog.** It gains a `delivery` field after `model`: `‹ configured ›`, `‹ local ›` or `‹ pr ›` (Right or Space forward, Left back).
  - It is sent as `StartGoal.delivery`; `configured` sends `None`.
  - The dialog has seven fields and one more row. Three expectations changed: Tab's order, the focus helper's bound, and the 80×24/120×40 render.
- **I1.** `run_title`'s fallback while delivering is now ` delivering · <age> `. That is never wider than the plain ` <m>/<n> merged · <age> `, because `delivering` is as wide as `<m>/<n> merged` with one-digit counts and narrower beyond them. So `delivering` shows at 80×24.
  - The audit's `run view delivering` fixtures now name `delivering` as their state word.
  - The narrow assertion in `the_run_view_title_says_delivering` changed to match.
- **m1.** Added `the_delivery_row_cleans_the_remote_and_the_repo`. Mutants that drop `clean` on the remote or on the repo each turn it red.
- **m2.** A stage row's CI mark is drawn in `ci_look`'s role, in both the graph and the compact list: `stage_pr::ci_mark_span` gives its byte range in the row text, which carries no host text, so nothing can forge the mark.
  - Pinned by `a_stage_rows_ci_mark_wears_its_role`. The audits still pass, because status roles are not the accent.
  - The stage inspector's `ci` row stays plain. Its marks sit among check names, which are host text, and a Rows field has no mark-position mechanism. Colouring by glyph would let a check named `✓` forge green.
- **m3.** `config::settings::{strip_hidden, clean_entry}` are now the one copy of the rule. `cleaned` and `settings_flow::drop_hidden` both use them, and `cleaned_is_the_shared_helpers` pins it.
- **m4.** `the_delivery_rows_draw_at_80x24` checks the rows, the bar and the editor's `‹ pr ›`/`< pr >`, in Unicode and ASCII.
- **Evidence.** Mutants, each restored from a copy:

#### Fix round 1 (task M9.2.14)

- **I1, the PR-open and pr-start bounds.** `support/run_pr.rs` gains two bounds, both built from public constants and never from a copy:
  - `PR_OPEN_WAIT` (625 s) = `RUN_WAIT` + 2 × the harness's `CHECK_TIMEOUT_SECS` (a new `pub const` in `run_harness.rs`, which its config now formats) + `PUSH_TIMEOUT` + 3 × `HOST_READ_TIMEOUT` + `HOST_WRITE_TIMEOUT` + one more `HOST_READ_TIMEOUT` (the first view) + 5 s (one poll at `poll_max_secs`, and scheduling). Every PR-open wait uses it: `wait_pr`, `wait_prs`, `wait_stage`, and `open_pr`'s `wait_run`.
  - `PR_START_WAIT` (395 s) = `REQUEST_WAIT` + `daemon::host::PREFLIGHT_BOUND`, applied through `pr_start` (`RunningCommand::finish`). Every `run start` in `run_cli_delivery.rs` goes through it.
  - `docs/timing-budgets.md`: the row is corrected, and a row for the start is added.
- **m1, sanitised output.** `status::printable` is now `proto::safe_text::multi_line`, which drops bidi and zero-width characters and makes U+2028/2029 a space. The text and `--json` of `run status`, `run start`'s stderr block, `run stats`, `run accept`'s moved-base listing and every prompt go through it, as do `run prs` and `fake-github`'s output.
  - One pinned expectation changed: `printed_daemon_text_has_no_control_characters` now expects a `\r\n` to become one `\n`, not a space and a `\n`. It also gained the bidi/ZWJ/BOM/U+2028 case.
  - New end-to-end test `run_output_drops_hidden_format_characters`: a planted goal (U+202E, U+200D) in `run status`, text and JSON, and an echoed run name in a refusal.
- **m2.** `fake-github create-repo` refuses a path that is not a bare repository under `--dir`. The check is a local `git -C <path> --no-optional-locks rev-parse --is-bare-repository --absolute-git-dir` with the five `GIT_*` variables removed: the answer must be `true`, and the git dir must be the path itself. The text is `fake-github: <path> is not a bare repository under <dir>` (invented). Tested with a bare repository outside `--dir`, a plain directory, the work repository, and `<bare>/objects`.
- **m3.** The panic hook installed before `fake-github` runs is restored, not the default.
- **m4.** `PrRig::env` sets `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=file`.
- **m5.** The comment now cites exit code 2 as the proof that the daemon was not reached.
- **Carried to M9.2.16** (ruling): binary-level tests of discard on a complete pr run, of accept refused on a complete pr run, and of `run deliver`'s accepted text.

### Task M9.2.16

- **What exists now.** `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_pr.rs` (576 lines, nine tests) and `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_pr_ci.rs` (354, four tests), through the real binary, a real daemon on `/tmp` paths and `FakeHost`. `support/run_pr.rs` (task 14's `PrRig`, 348 lines) gains `PrRig::unscripted` (the bare remote and `origin`, a fake GitHub with no repository and no login), `bare_git`, `remote_refs`, `calls_of`, `contains`, `wait_stage_within`, `pr_harness_with(orchestrator, files, extra_toml, deciders)` and `pr_harness_unscripted`, and the derived bounds `VIEW_WAIT`, `PUSH_WAIT`, `FETCH_WAIT`, `RERUN_WAIT`, `CI_FIX_WAIT` and `FIX_PUSH_WAIT` (`docs/timing-budgets.md`, "Recorded, from M9.2.16").
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **Two files, not one.** The brief names `run_e2e_pr.rs` only; the CI scenarios are in `run_e2e_pr_ci.rs` so neither passes 600 lines (AGENTS.md rule 8).
  2. **Files the brief lists but this task did not need to touch.** `PrRig` already existed (task 14), and so did the `gh` pin in `pin_agents` and `run_harness.rs` (task 12's fix round, m1). The harness's environment already reaches every start, so no `extra_env` was added. `support/mod.rs` and `run_harness.rs` are unchanged.
  3. **The carried tests** (ruling, task 14's fix round). `e2e_pr_run_accept_and_discard_are_refused` checks both refusals on a running `pr` run. Then the user merges the PR (`FakeGithubCtl::merge`) and the run completes. On the complete run, `run accept` is still refused, and `run discard` (ruling R-1) succeeds. That discard removes the local branches and `refs/remotes/origin/anthrex/<run>/` (ruling 11), leaves the remote's refs and the merged PR as they were, and leaves `calls.jsonl` unchanged. `run deliver`'s accepted text has its own test, `e2e_pr_run_deliver_requests_tier3_before_the_pr_opens`: a check that, in `.full` only, waits for the test (at most 7 s, inside the 10 s check timeout) holds tier 3 in flight. `run deliver --stage 1` then answers `stage 1: tier 3 requested; its PR opens when tier 3 is green`, and, once the PR is open, `stage 1's PR is already open: <url>`.
  4. **The rewritten branch's text is the "moved" one** (rulings on task 8's concern 3 and its fix round): `remote stage branch anthrex/<run>/stage-1 moved: someone else pushed to it or rewrote it; stage 1 (PR #1) is at <r7> on origin, which does not contain <p7>, and anthrex never forces a push`. The log line is Messages' exact `stage 1 (PR #1): origin has <r7>, which does not contain <p7>`. To prove that nothing was forced, the bare repository logs every ref update (`core.logAllRefUpdates = always`, set before the run). After the halt, the stage branch is still the rewrite, and its reflog holds exactly anthrex's one push and then the rewrite. No `gh` call names a push, a force or a `+` refspec.
  5. **Preflight's cases.** `gh` missing runs on the real `GhHost` (`ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=gh`, the pinned `/nonexistent/anthrex-test/gh`; no PATH lookup). The other six run on one daemon over `pr_harness_unscripted`, set up in turn: `remote.origin.url` unset; a GitLab URL; the fake not logged in; logged in, with no repository; the repository created, with `pushInsteadOf` pointed at a path that does not exist; and the base branch deleted from the bare repository. Each text is exact, check 5 being task 12's sentence with git's quoted `and the repository exists.`. After each refusal there is no run, no local `anthrex/` branch or `refs/anthrex/` ref, no remote `anthrex/` branch and no PR.
  6. **The bisect scenario's profile has no `check`** (finding F1 below). It is 9.1's tiered repository with one `single_test` change: `one.sh` fails `<module>::ci` while `mods/<module>/CI_FAIL` exists. The module tests of tiers 1 and 2 never read that marker. The CI rule is red when `mods/b/CI_FAIL` contains `bad`, which `t2` writes. The scripted `ci_summary` answers `b::ci` (fake-agent's decider scripts are `{"answer": …}`). The bisect blames `t2`, and `fix1` (origin `ci`, `t2`'s `owns`) removes the marker; its merge reaches PR 1, whose CI goes green. Stage 2's task waits, as `run_e2e_stages.rs`'s does, until the fix is on PR 1. So stage 2's PR opens on a head that already holds the fix (propagated) and is green, and the run has one CI fix task and one bisect line.
  7. **Briefs are read from `run.json`.** The snapshot's `TaskInfo.brief` is empty for these tasks.
- **Finding F1, for the controller (design; not changed here).** In `pr` mode a reproduced CI red on the head a PR opened with is never bisected while the profile has a `check`:
  - Decision 19 opens a PR only once tier 3 is green on its head, so `StageFull.green_at` is that head.
  - Decision 27 step 4 hands the red to 9.1's `bisect::start`, whose range (decision 36) is the merges after the last green tier 3. That range is empty.
  - The red becomes a stage fix (`Repro::Reproduced`, no culprit). The log reads `stage 1: CI red at <sha7> reproduces; not bisected: <sha7> is not a merge recorded on the stage's line`. That reason is misleading: the head is on the line; the range after it is empty.
  - Only a red on a later head (a fix or a propagate pushed after the open, which passed tier 2 only) can be bisected.
  - The test reaches the bisect with an unverified tiered profile, which decision 19 opens without tier 3.
  - Whether a CI bisect should start from the PR's base (or its last CI-green head) instead of the last local tier-3 green is for the controller.
- **Rule-6 evidence.** Tasks 1–15 had already built the behaviour, so every test passed on its first green run, except two that failed on the test's own setup and were corrected: the decider script's shape, and briefs read from the snapshot. Four mutants, each applied alone to a copy of one daemon file, run against its test and restored, are RED:
  - M1, `cancel_outcome` never lists the open PRs: `e2e_pr_cancel_leaves_prs_open` failed in 8.5 s.
  - M2, an adopt's `NotDescendant` treated as `Adopted` (the rewrite taken): `e2e_pr_rewritten_remote_stage_branch_halts_without_forcing` timed out after its 372 s bound with the run still `running`.
  - M3, cancelled-only CI not taken as infra: `e2e_pr_ci_infra_failure_is_rerun_once` failed, with no re-run (`left: 0, right: 1`).
  - M4, R-1 undone (discard refused on a complete run): `e2e_pr_run_accept_and_discard_are_refused` failed with decision 39's text.
- **GREEN.** `cargo test -p anthrex --test run_e2e_pr --test run_e2e_pr_ci` passed 9 + 4 tests in 12.5 s + 28 s. Three copies of each binary at once also passed every test (40 s, 55 s). `anthrex-daemon --test test_rigs_pin_agents` passed 2. `cargo fmt --all --check` and `cargo +1.99 clippy -p anthrex --all-targets -- -D warnings`, on the host and on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, were clean. Every `PrRig` drop found `forbidden.jsonl` empty.
- **The smoke stage's fact** (for M9.2.17, not touched here). `fake-github create-repo` refuses anything but a bare repository under `--dir`, so the smoke stage must create its bare remote inside the fake's directory.

#### Fix round 1 (task M9.2.16)

- **I1, the deliver test's window.**
  - The gate now holds tier 3 for `go` up to `GATE_SECS` = `REQUEST_WAIT + 30s` (90 s). The test's `run deliver` is one request, sent after one 100 ms poll, and the 30 s covers that poll and the client's spawn.
  - The plan's `check_timeout_secs` is `GATE_SECS + 30` (120 s). It sits above the gate's deadline, so a held tier 3 never times out red.
  - `go` is written by a drop guard (`OpenGate`), so a test that fails before it opens the gate leaves no tier 3 waiting.
  - The `docs/timing-budgets.md` row is re-derived from these bounds.
  - Probe: a 15 s pause before `run deliver` (twice the old 7 s window, and beyond the old 10 s check timeout) passes.
- **m1.** When `fix1` appears, `t3` is already `working` on stage 2's line. Once stage 2's PR is open, the run's log holds `stage 2: propagated stage 1 at <fix1's merge>`. Together these prove the fix reached stage 2 by a propagate.
- **m2.** `run_harness.rs` names its `git_timeout_secs` as `GIT_TIMEOUT_SECS`, and `run_pr.rs` uses it.
- **m3.** `e2e_pr_ci_red_reproduced_under_a_check_is_a_stage_fix_without_a_bisect` is the bisect scenario's stage 1 under a profile with a `check`. It asserts:
  - the stage fix: the stage's `owns`, and "No single task's merge is the cause.";
  - no bisect line;
  - the current log text `stage 1: CI red at <h7> reproduces; not bisected: <h7> is not a merge recorded on the stage's line`, pinned under a `RULING F1` comment so the final wave moves the pin with the text.
- **m4.** A comment says that `calls.jsonl` records `gh` argv only, and that the reflog is the push proof.
- **m5.** The `gh`-missing harness sets `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=file`.
- **Shared helpers.** The two bisect-path tests share their setup through `marker_ci` and `ci_files`.
- **Line counts.** `run_e2e_pr.rs` 599, `run_e2e_pr_ci.rs` 435, `run_pr.rs` 473.

### Task M9.2.17

- **What exists now.** `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_pr_review.rs` (four tests) and `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_pr_land.rs` (five), through the real binary, a real daemon on `/tmp` paths and `FakeHost`. `support/run_pr.rs` gains `pr_run` (a plan start in `pr` mode), `orch_pr_harness` (M9's planned-run harness on a `PrRig`), `pr_goal`, `log_lines`, `wait_log_line`, and the derived bounds `COMMENT_WAIT`, `REPLY_WAIT`, `LAND_WAIT` and `PR_GOAL_WAIT` (`docs/timing-budgets.md`, "Recorded, from M9.2.17"). Smoke stage 11i is `scripts/pty_smoke_pr.py::pr_stage`.
- **Deviations and readings** (none changes a design decision):
  1. **`docs/ROADMAP.md` is not touched.** The controller sets 9.2 to `done` after the whole-branch review (the dispatch's instruction, over the brief's file list).
  2. **Planned runs.** `orch_pr_harness` starts M9's orchestrator harness (`RunHarness::orch`), then sets up the `PrRig` (which needs the repository) and restarts the harness's own daemon on it through `RunHarness::restart_daemon` (its own socket, else its own child). The review-batch test's orchestrator reads `run_status` after the wake, captures the two thread refs from the `delivery` block, and adds `rv1` with `addresses` (a planner cannot take a `fix<n>` id). The closed-PR test is a planned run too, so the wake text can be checked word for word in what the orchestrator read.
  3. **"No wake" on a fast-path run.** The non-writer and restart tests run without an orchestrator (decision 31's fast path), where no wake line can exist. They assert instead that no `review batch` log line appears (a closed batch is the wake's only source), and the non-writer test also that the run has no orchestrator.
  4. **The worker's recorded prompt** is the first stream-json user message `fake-agent` read on its stdin (`FAKE_AGENT_STDIN_FILE`, `<io>/worker-fix1-1.stdin`). The test finds the one label line, reads the fence after it (all backticks, longer than the comment's longest run, 4), checks the text between it and the closing fence is the comment verbatim, and that none of `owns: Cargo.toml`, `ignore previous instructions` or `gh pr merge` appears anywhere else in the prompt.
  5. **The base sync's two parents.** The brief says the stage's new head has two parents, the base and the old head. Observed: the sync task's own commit (9.1's hand-back, `MERGE_HEAD` the base) has exactly those parents, `(old head, base)`, and the stage's new head is M8a's merge-queue merge of that task, `(old head, sync commit)`. The test asserts both. "Never a rebase": the bare remote logs every ref update (`core.logAllRefUpdates = always`), and each update of the stage branch descends from the one before.
  6. **The squash scenario** deletes the branch, so GitHub retargets PR 2 itself; anthrex's own `pr edit 2 --base main` follows as the harmless repeat Risks describes, and the test waits for it in `calls.jsonl`. The synced head's tree equals the old head's (no file change) and its parents are `(old head, squash commit)`.
  7. **Completion** runs on 9.1's tiered repository (`tier_repo_files`, `tier_plan`) with `[testing] full_idle_secs = 3600`, so 9.1's idle trigger cannot start a tier 3 during the test and only a completion could. Tier 3 ran twice before the merges (`check.sh` in `.full`, once per stage before its PR opened). After the second merge neither the count nor any stage's tier-3 record (`run.json`'s `stages[].full`) changes, although stage 2's synced head has no tier 3 of its own. Both PRs are merged with merge commits, so both `stage` lines read `merge_method: merge`.
  8. **The restart test** waits for the run to show `paused` after `restart_daemon` (M8a), resumes it with `anthrex run resume`, waits for two more `pr view` calls, then checks the task list, the thread's replies (one), the `POST` calls, the re-runs, the log reads and the batch lines are unchanged.
  9. **Smoke stage 11i.**
     - It runs its own daemon under one `/tmp/anthrex-smoke-pr-*` directory (socket, data, `ANTHREX_CONFIG`, the fake's directory with the bare remote inside it) and stops it in `finally`, also on failure (checked: the first, failing, run left no daemon and no directory).
     - `ANTHREX_GH_BIN=/nonexistent/anthrex-smoke/gh`, `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=fake` and `ANTHREX_FAKE_HOST_DIR` are set in the stage's environment only; the script's `ENV` keeps task 12's `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=gh` and `/nonexistent/anthrex-test/gh`.
     - `pty-smoke.py` gains, beyond the import and the call: an `env` argument on `PtyProc` (the stage's TUI must attach to its own daemon) and `ANTHREX_SMOKE_ONLY=11i`, which runs that stage alone (the script had no stage selector; only a stage that needs nothing earlier stages set up may run alone).
     - Stage 2's task waits for a file the stage writes once PR 1's CI is green, so PR 2 opens on a head that already holds the CI fix (the technique of `run_e2e_pr_ci.rs`).
     - The brief names only the user's squash merge of stage 1; the run completes only when every PR has landed, so the stage also merges stage 2 (a merge commit) once it is retargeted to `main`.
     - The run view's rows are checked as `stage <n>/2  tier 3 … #<n>  ci ✓`. Stage 1's tier-3 mark is not asserted: after its CI fix the head moved, and tier 3 does not run on an open PR's updates (decision 28), so it reads `◌` (filed in the follow-ups for M9.5).
- **Rule-6 evidence.** Tasks 1–16 had already built the behaviour, so every test passed on its first green run, except where the test's own setup was wrong (a reply asserted before its wait; the single-stage run's local branch is `integration`; the smoke's row text). Nine mutants, each applied alone to a byte copy of one daemon file, run against its test and restored from the copy, are RED:
  - M1, every commenter writes (`review::writes` answers `Some(true)`): the non-writer test, no `ignored … no write access` line within 106 s.
  - M2, a comment quoted without its fence (`quote::comment`): the injected-comment test, the line after the label is the comment's first line, not a fence.
  - M3, a fix outside the stage never held (`review_fix::hold_outside`): the unowned-file test, no hold line within 106 s.
  - M4, one parent read as a merge commit (`land.rs`): the squash test, its `stage` line reads `merge`.
  - M5, a closed PR pauses nothing (`land::repause`): the closed test, stage 2 not paused within 67 s.
  - M6, a restart loses the watermark (`restore::prepare` clears each stage's threads and watermark): the restart test, the thread got a second reply.
  - M7, a closed batch wakes nobody (`review.rs`): the review-batch test, the orchestrator's first message is another wake (the batch fell to the fast path, whose unscripted `fix1` blocked).
  - M8, a landed `pr` run still runs 9.1's tier-3 completion (`complete.rs`'s R-8 arm): the completion test, stage 2's tier-3 record moved after the last merge. M8 first survived against the test as first written (an untiered profile, where 9.1's completion is inactive and the final check is skipped on an already-verified head), so the test was rewritten on 9.1's tiered repository with `full_idle_secs = 3600`. It also survived a count of `check.sh` runs in `.full`: the tier 3 it started was answered by 9.1's result cache (stage 2's synced tree is one tier 3 already passed), so the test compares the stages' tier-3 records too.
  - M9, the sync task's title (`templates::sync_title`): the base-conflict test, at the title.
- **GREEN.** `cargo test -p anthrex --test run_e2e_pr_review --test run_e2e_pr_land --test run_e2e_pr --test run_e2e_pr_ci` passed 4 + 5 + 9 + 4 tests (17 s, 27 s, 13 s, 26 s); `anthrex-daemon --test test_rigs_pin_agents` passed 2. `ANTHREX_SMOKE_ONLY=11i python3 scripts/pty-smoke.py` passed in 29 s (twice; the first, failing, run left no daemon and no `/tmp` directory). `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo +1.99 clippy -p anthrex --all-targets -- -D warnings` on the host and on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, and `cargo +1.98 check -p anthrex --tests` were clean. Every `PrRig` drop found `forbidden.jsonl` empty. The full suite and the full smoke run are the controller's.
- **Follow-up filed** (M9.5): an open PR's stage node reads `tier 3 ◌` once a fix moves its head.
- **Fix round 1** (the controller's rulings on the review):
  - *I1, the stage's daemon is always stopped.* The daemon is now the stage's own child: `daemon start --foreground` through `subprocess.Popen`, as the Rust harness starts its daemons. The stage waits for its socket within `DAEMON_START_WAIT`. A detached `daemon start` that fails or times out can leave a daemon that binds its socket only after the cleanup has looked. That was proven, not assumed: a scratch copy forcing `timeout=0.3` on the old detached start left pid 71466 running, which was stopped through its own socket with `anthrex daemon stop` under its variables, never a signal. The `finally` (`_stop_daemon`) now runs `anthrex daemon stop` under the stage's variables whenever its socket exists, until the child it spawned has exited. It allows at most 60 + 40 s, then reports a pid that did not exit. Proof, on scratch copies of the scripts:
    - a forced `fail` after the daemon bound its socket (pid 72071);
    - a forced `fail` right after the spawn, before it bound (pid 72159).

    Each left `pgrep -fl "anthrex daemon"` at the user's 48989, 66785 and 67968, and no `/tmp/anthrex-smoke-pr-*` directory.
  - *m2.* A `daemon stop` that times out (`SystemExit` from `run_cmd`'s `fail`) is reported, and the cleanup goes on: the wait, then the `rmtree`.
  - *m1.* `PrRig::quiet_views` replaces "two more views" in the non-writer, review-batch and restart tests:
    - it waits for three more views (the second answered);
    - then `BATCH_QUIET` (`review_batch_secs` plus one tick, derived);
    - then a view that starts after the quiet.

    It is bounded by `QUIET_VIEWS_WAIT` (timing-budgets row). The review-batch and restart tests also assert that the stage's `/threads` counts are unchanged. Mutant M6 is now RED at that assertion (`new: 1`), and M1 is still RED.
  - *m3.* Every message the worker read is scanned (`outside_quotes`): each labelled quote must be a fence longer than 4 backticks around the comment verbatim, and the injected texts may appear nowhere else.
  - *m4.* `pty_smoke_run.py` gains `git_env(extra)`, which `_git` uses. `subprocess_ok` reuses it (so it gets `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`), and the setup push passes `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=file`.
  - *m5.* `open_two` lost its unused `profile` parameter.
  - *m6.* Both wake texts are asserted whole: `[anthrex] Run <id> changed: <line>. Call run_status for the details.`
  - *m7.* `LAND_WAIT`'s last term is `RETARGET_WAIT`, one write plus the margin.
  - *Runs.* Both files passed: `run_e2e_pr_review` 4 in 22 s and `run_e2e_pr_land` 5 in 27 s. `ANTHREX_SMOKE_ONLY=11i` passed in 31 s. fmt and `+1.99` clippy (host and Linux) are clean.

## Final fix wave: engine (W1)

The controller's rulings in `.superpowers/sdd/M9.2-pr-delivery/fix-wave.md`, part W1, on review B (and A1, A4 of review A). Every item's red, green and mutant evidence is in `.superpowers/sdd/M9.2-pr-delivery/fix-wave-W1-report.md`.

- **I-1, a push answered after the user's merge.** What a PR delivered is the head the host reports at the merge: `PrRecord.watermark.head` of the view that saw it merged (`pushed_head` only when no view ever reported a head). `land::merged` counts the local stage head as delivered when it is that head, or when it is `PrRecord.confirmed`, the newest head a view of the still-open PR showed equal to the pushed head (set at the open, then by each such view; a PR head only fast-forwards, so what an open view showed is in the merge). Otherwise `unpushed` runs, its texts reworded so they hold for a pushed-but-late head too: `…: merged at <at7>, without <head7>, so its commits go up with stage <m>`, and at the top `stage <n> PR #<k> was merged at <at7>, without <head7>; that work is not delivered (anthrex run cancel gives up)`. A reply is ready only once a view of the open PR shows the pushed head: `ReplyDue.push_done` marks a reply whose push was answered (an adopt answers it too), and `reply::confirmed` makes those ready on the view. At the merge every automatic reply that is not ready is ready when the head was delivered, or when its own push was the merged head; any other is dropped, the thread stays `tasked`, and the attention line `PR #<k> was merged at <at7> before fix task <id> reached it: the fix missed the merge, so thread <pr>:<key> gets no reply` (invented; key `<n>/missed`, an attention line only, not a typed alert) is raised. Reading: a user who pushes twice on GitHub and merges before anthrex views the first push is reported as not delivered, because the engine (pure) cannot ask git whether the merged head contains the local one; recorded as a follow-up. The e2e reply tests now see each reply one poll later (the view after the push).
- **I-2, duplicate CI fix tasks.** `ci::classified` looks, once the key is known and before the cap, for an unfinished `FixOf::Ci` task of the stage with that key. With one, the red adds nothing: its record goes, the fix's record keeps the red's head in the new `CiRecord.newest` (or, when that record was trimmed, the red's own record stands for it, `Tasked` on that fix), and the log says `stage <n>: CI red at <sha7> is the failure fix task <id> is still fixing; CI is judged again when it finishes` (invented). Each pass (`ci::finished`), a record whose `newest` is set and whose fix has finished clears it and, when the stage head is still the pushed head (no newer push is coming), calls `ci_trigger::rearm`: the seen marker of the pushed head and the finished records on it go, and the PR is due a view now, so the red there raises its fix or its alert again. `ci_fix_max` counts the key's fix tasks that were not cancelled (a cancelled fix made no fix; it is what I-3's re-armed reds need). Reading: the reproduction and the decider still run for the joining red, since its key is the decider's answer. `delivery_ci_cap.rs::round` now merges each round's fix before the next red, as the cap's scenario means.
